# Changelog — Internal Development History

This file documents all public and internal development iterations of DashCast.
See [README.md](README.md) for the project overview and installation instructions.

---

## Stable releases

| Version | versionCode | Summary |
|---------|-------------|---------|
| **1.0.1** | 155 | **Bug fix — cluster screen-size selector in Settings was not clickable.** In the Settings screen, the three cluster-type options (`8.8"`, `12.3"`, `10.25"`) lived inside a `RadioGroup` (`rg_cluster_type`), but the `MaterialRadioButton` widgets had `android:clickable="false"` and `android:focusable="false"`, and the surrounding `LinearLayout` rows had **no id, no click handler, and no selectable background**. Because the radio buttons are nested inside another `LinearLayout` (not direct children of the `RadioGroup`), `RadioGroup`'s built-in auto-check mechanism never received any tap event either — so the entire selection was inert: the user could not change the saved size. Fix: each of the three rows now has its own id (`row_cluster_88`, `row_cluster_123`, `row_cluster_1025`), `android:clickable="true"`, `android:focusable="true"` and `android:background="?attr/selectableItemBackground"` (Material ripple). In `SettingsActivity.wireListeners()`, each row delegates its click to `rgClusterType.check(R.id.rb_XX)`, which in turn triggers the existing `OnCheckedChangeListener` that persists the corresponding sendInfo command (29 = 8.8" / 30 = 12.3" Seal EU / 31 = 10.25") into `cluster_screen_size_cmd` in SharedPreferences. The downstream wiring is preserved end-to-end: long-pressing the **Stop projection** button in `MainActivity` calls `originCluster()`, which reads `getClusterTypeCmd()` and forwards it to `AdbLocalClient.restoreOriginCluster(ctx, screenSizeCmd, pkg, cb)`, so the user-selected cluster size is correctly applied when restoring the native BYD instrument cluster. No business logic was modified — only XML attributes on the three rows and three click handlers in `SettingsActivity`. |
| **1.0.0** | 154 | **Official stable release** — exit from the alpha/beta channel after extensive in-car validation on BYD Seal EU (DiLink 3.0). Consolidates the entire 0.9.x M3 redesign, Settings/Welcome i18n pass, OTA flow, long-press "Restore Origin Cluster" bottom-sheet, audit lint/javac cleanup (lint 169→71, javac warnings 49→0) and the 12-locale documentation rebuild. |

## Pre-releases

| Version | versionCode | Summary |
|---------|-------------|---------|
| **1.2.50-beta** | 308 | **Diagnostic — Voice tab étape 2/3 : wake-word placeholder via openWakeWord ONNX.** Suite logique de v1.2.43 (chaîne audio validée sur Seal EU 25/05/2026). On branche le pipeline openWakeWord complet (mel → embedding → wake) sur le flux PCM 16 kHz déjà capté par `VoiceService`, en restant strictement isolé de la prod. **Modèle utilisé.** `hey_jarvis_v0.1.onnx` (release openWakeWord v0.5.1, ~1.2 MB) — **placeholder** pour le futur `ok_dashcast` qu'on entraînera plus tard. Pourquoi : le but de cette étape n'est PAS de détecter « Ok DashCast » mais de valider end-to-end sur du vrai matériel BYD que (a) ONNX Runtime Android (1.17.1) charge et exécute 3 sessions sans crash, (b) le mel→embed→wake produit des scores cohérents en streaming, (c) le coût CPU sur Seal EU reste tenable pour un service de fond. Une fois ces 3 points cochés, on aura confiance pour entraîner et déployer le modèle custom. **Pipeline (mirror openwakeword/utils.py).** Tampon coulissant 2,5 s de PCM int16 → toutes les ~100 ms : (1) `melspectrogram.onnx` sur la fenêtre complète → mel features `(n_frames, 32)` avec normalisation `x/10 + 2`, (2) batch de 16 inférences `embedding_model.onnx` sur des fenêtres de 76 frames stride 8 → `(16, 96)` (Google speech_embedding 96-dim), (3) `hey_jarvis_v0.1.onnx` sur ce tenseur `(1, 16, 96)` → score sigmoïde `[0..1]`. Seuil de détection = 0.5, cool-down 1.5 s entre deux émissions. Le tout sur un thread worker dédié (`wakeword-engine`, prio NORM-1) avec `setIntraOpNumThreads(1) + setInterOpNumThreads(1)` pour ne pas affamer la pipeline cluster-mirror. **Isolation prod (verbatim).** Le code wake-word vit intégralement dans `com.byd.dashcast.voice.wakeword.*` (un fichier : `WakeWordEngine.java`, ~350 LoC). `VoiceService` reçoit un mini hook non invasif : un champ `static volatile SampleConsumer sSampleConsumer`, **null en prod**, set uniquement quand l'utilisateur active le switch Voice → Wake word dans Diag. Hot path inchangé quand le champ est null → zéro régression versus v1.2.43. Tout `OrtSession.run` est wrappé en try/catch ; sur échec d'init, l'engine bascule en mode dégradé et le panneau affiche « ONNX Runtime indisponible — &lt;cause&gt; ». **APK delta.** Assets ONNX dans `app/src/main/assets/voice/wakeword/` : `melspectrogram.onnx` 1.0 MB + `embedding_model.onnx` 1.3 MB + `hey_jarvis_v0.1.onnx` 1.2 MB = 3.5 MB. Native libs : `com.microsoft.onnxruntime:onnxruntime-android:1.17.1` ~25 MB toutes ABI, donc nouvelle gradle `defaultConfig.ndk.abiFilters = ['arm64-v8a', 'armeabi-v7a']` (les seules ABI sur lesquelles DiLink tourne) qui rabat à ~8 MB de natif. Total +~12 MB. **UI.** Nouvelle MaterialCardView « Mot d'éveil (openWakeWord) » sous le VU-mètre du panneau Voice : `MaterialSwitch` (default OFF — l'engine est créé lazy, zéro coût pour qui n'active jamais le switch), `ProgressBar` 0..1000 pour le score sigmoïde × 1000, ligne « Dernière détection : HH:mm:ss (score X.XX) », ligne modèle « hey_jarvis_v0.1 (placeholder pour Ok DashCast) ». Le switch déclenche `WakeWordEngine.start()` + `VoiceService.setSampleConsumer(engine)` ; l'OFF fait l'inverse + nullifie le consumer. **Broadcast contract.** Nouvelle action `com.byd.dashcast.voice.WAKEWORD` (extras `EXTRA_WW_SCORE` float, `EXTRA_WW_LAST_MS` long, `EXTRA_WW_LABEL` string, `EXTRA_WW_UNAVAILABLE` bool, `EXTRA_WW_REASON` string), broadcast in-process via `LocalBroadcastManager` — pas d'IPC supplémentaire, pas de perm supplémentaire. `DiagActivity.voiceReceiver` étendu pour dispatcher la nouvelle action vers `onWakeWordBroadcast`. **i18n.** 7 nouvelles clés × 12 locales = 84 entrées (`diag_voice_wakeword_card_title` / `_switch` / `_score_label` / `_model_fmt` / `_last_fmt` / `_last_at_fmt` / `_unavailable`), FR + EN curated à la main + 10 autres locales injectées via script idempotent. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (307→308, 1.2.49-beta→1.2.50-beta + commentaire détaillé + `ndk.abiFilters` + dep ONNX Runtime), `voice/VoiceService.java` (+ interface nested `SampleConsumer`, +`sSampleConsumer` field + `setSampleConsumer`/`getSampleConsumer` + 5-ligne hook dans captureLoop), nouveau `voice/wakeword/WakeWordEngine.java`, `res/layout/include_diag_voice.xml` (+1 MaterialCardView), `DiagActivity.java` (+ 4 fields wake-word, + extension `voiceReceiver`, + `onWakeWordToggle`, + `onWakeWordBroadcast`, + cleanup `onDestroy`), `res/values*/strings.xml` × 12 (+7 keys each), nouveau dir `assets/voice/wakeword/` avec 3 ONNX. **Zero behavioural regression.** Aucun touch à MainActivity / ClusterMirrorManager / MirrorTestRunner / fission tiers / Settings / sniffer / mirror daemon. Switch OFF par défaut. Engine init lazy. Échec ONNX → UI dégradée propre, jamais de crash. **Behavioural contract.** Toute plateforme — install v1.2.50 par-dessus v1.2.49, ouvrir Diagnostic → Voice (alpha), cliquer **Démarrer l'écoute** (popup RECORD_AUDIO si pas encore accordé), basculer le switch « Mot d'éveil » sur ON : la `ProgressBar` Score se met à respirer entre 0 et quelques % selon le bruit ambiant. Dire « Hey Jarvis » à voix normale devant le micro : le score grimpe au-dessus de 0.5, la ligne « Dernière détection » s'incrémente. Couper le switch ou arrêter l'écoute : engine stoppé, ressources ONNX libérées. Si ONNX Runtime échoue à se charger (ABI exotique, OOM, asset corrompu…), le panneau passe en mode « ONNX Runtime indisponible » sans toucher au reste de l'app. Étape 3/3 (entraînement custom `ok_dashcast` + intégration Vosk pour l'ASR post-wake) arrivera sur la lancée de cette validation. |
| **1.2.49-beta** | 307 | **DL5 Fission task-id extraction hardening.** Third field test (`log/byd_report_20260525_152233.log`, 11 PASS / 1 WARN / 0 FAIL / 4 SKIPPED) was the first run where the new interactive multi-display probe worked end-to-end: F01 opened the cluster projection, F04 launched Yandex on display 2 (hidden, the testeur correctly answered NO), F06 launched it on display 3, F07's popup got a clean **YES** and the runner recorded display 3 as the cluster. Only one bug remained: the v1.2.48 awk-scoped `extractYandexTaskIdOnDisplay` returned empty on this ROM (the actual `dumpsys activity activities` layout does not match the `(mPreferredTopFocusableRootTask\|mLastFocusedRootTask)=Task{…A=…yandexmaps}` pattern that v1.2.47/48 inherited from the v1.2.46 ROM dumps), so `yandexTaskIdOnCluster = -1` → F10 WARN « no taskId extracted for confirmed display 3 » → F11 SKIPPED « no taskId (F10) » → F12 SKIPPED « no taskId from F10 ». The actual resize therefore never ran. **Root-cause analysis.** Two fragile assumptions: (a) scoping the task to a specific displayId via dump structure is ROM-dependent and we have no way to know which DiLink5 firmwares ship which layout without burning a field test for each; (b) the task may not be fully registered in the dump at the exact moment the user taps YES, especially if Yandex has been bouncing between displays in the previous candidate steps. **Fix.** Drop the per-display scoping entirely — since the runner force-stops Yandex before every candidate launch (F04/F06/F08 all start with `am force-stop`), exactly ONE Yandex task exists at the moment of YES. So a global match for `Task{…ru.yandex.yandexmaps…}` is sufficient and ROM-agnostic. The new extractor `extractYandexTaskId(Context, StringBuilder debugOut)` does: (1) a primary regex `Task\{[^}]*ru\.yandex\.yandexmaps[^}]*\}` then `#(\d+)` on the matched substring, (2) a fallback regex `taskId=(\d+)[^\n]*ru\.yandex\.yandexmaps` for ROMs that format tasks differently, (3) up to **4 attempts** with 0 / 250 / 500 / 1000 ms backoff so a late-registered task is still caught (covers the case where the Yandex SplashScreen → main activity transition hasn't published the task at the exact YES tap). The dumpsys read is filtered through `grep -nE 'Task\{|taskId=|yandexmaps' | grep -i yandex | head -20` so the captured debug stays compact (≤ 20 lines per attempt). On success the extractor returns the integer taskId; on failure it returns -1 and the full per-attempt debug capture (raw filtered dumpsys lines) is appended to `r.detail` of F07 (and re-captured in F10 if the prompt-time extraction had failed and F10 retries). This means **any future taskId extraction regression is debuggable from the field report alone** — no adb access required, no need to re-instrument the build. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (306→307, 1.2.48-beta→1.2.49-beta + per-version comment block), `dilink5/Dl5ClusterReconRunner.java` (one method replaced — `extractYandexTaskIdOnDisplay(Context,int)` → `extractYandexTaskId(Context, StringBuilder)`; two call sites updated — `fissionPromptDisplay` and `fissionSetFreeformOnCluster` now both pass a StringBuilder and emit the debug capture into their respective `r.detail`; ~80 LoC net change, no API change outside the file). No new permission, no manifest change, no new string resource, no new layout, no new dependency, no DiagActivity change, no R-tier change. **Behavioural contract.** DL5 testeur — install v1.2.49 over v1.2.48, open Diagnostic → Cluster DL5 → **Test Fission** → Confirm. Same exact UX as v1.2.48 (same projection activation, same per-display launch + Oui/Non popup, same NO bascule). Difference: when the testeur answers **Oui** on the cluster display, F07/F09 now reliably picks up the Yandex taskId (with up to 1.75 s of retry budget if needed), and **F10/F11/F12 actually run** — F10 forces the task into freeform via `cmd activity set-task-windowing-mode <tid> 5`, F11 resizes it to 100×80 → 1820×640, F12 verifies the new bounds. The exported report contains a new `--- task lookup debug ---` block under F07 with the raw dumpsys lines the extractor used; if extraction ever fails on a future ROM, that block plus the visible per-attempt output is sufficient to design the next fix without a roundtrip. F13/F14/F15/F16 cleanup unchanged. **Safety.** Strictly scoped to one method + its two call sites in the F-tier extraction path. Non-DL5 platforms see no behavioural difference (single SKIPPED row preserved). R-tier listener untouched. Popup logic, i18n keys, version-independent infra all untouched. |
| **1.2.48-beta** | 306 | **DL5 Fission runner — interactive multi-display probe + projection auto-activation.** Second field test (`log/byd_report_20260525_144058.log`, 9 PASS / 2 WARN / 1 FAIL) showed two distinct shortcomings of the v1.2.47 runner: (a) the awk parser matched the FIRST `A=…yandexmaps` occurrence in `dumpsys activity activities`, which on this ROM sits in the flat task list BEFORE any `displayId=` line — so `DISP=` came out empty and F06 was a false-negative FAIL while F10 was a false-negative WARN (Yandex actually had landed on display 3 in F05, then moved back to display 0 in F09 correctly). (b) Tasks launched on fission displays always end up `mode=fullscreen` even with `--windowingMode 5` because the display itself declares `mDisplayWindowingMode=fullscreen`, so the later `cmd activity task resize` is silently a no-op. Rather than keep chasing dumpsys heuristics to guess which fission display is the cluster, v1.2.48 rewrites the F-tier as an **interactive** probe that **asks the user**, **on each candidate display in turn**, whether Yandex is visible on the cluster screen — per the testeur's explicit request: « j'aimerais bien que le test fission active la projection Dilink5 et tente de lancer l'application sur le display 2 puis 3 puis 4. À chaque fois je voudrais : l'application se lance sur le display X puis pop up à l'utilisateur dans sa langue qui demande si l'application est bien sur le cluster. Si il répond oui, on passe aux test de resize etc de l'application. Si il répond non, on bascule l'app sur le display 0, on la tue et on la relance sur le display 3 pour refaire le même process et ainsi de suite ». **New F-tier flow (16 steps, ~30–90 s typical depending on user answers).** **F01** opens the BYD cluster projection itself (`service call auto_container 2 i32 1000 i32 16 s16 ""`, same call documented in `MirrorTestRunner.runM10`) so fission displays become routable; sleeps 2 s to let the compositor wire them. Sets `FissionState.projectionOpenedByUs = true` so F16 knows whether to tear down. **F02** unchanged from v1.2.47 — resolves Yandex Maps launcher activity via `getLaunchIntentForPackage`. **F03** unchanged — `am compat enable 174042936 ru.yandex.yandexmaps`. **F04 / F06 / F08** force-stop Yandex (so the new compat takes effect) then `am start --display N --windowingMode 5 -n <component>` for N in {2, 3, 4} respectively. Stdout is parsed for « unable to resolve » / « does not exist » / « invalid display » / « no display » → FAIL / WARN with display-specific message; success records `lastLaunchedDisplay` and sleeps 2.5 s so the user has time to look at the cluster. Each step SKIPs cleanly if a previous candidate was already confirmed by the user. **F05 / F07 / F09** are the new interactive prompts. The runner posts `Listener.onPromptYesNo(title, message, callback)` on the UI thread (new default method on `Dl5ClusterReconRunner.Listener` — non-DL5 listeners and the R-tier path keep working without changes, default = NO) and blocks on a `CountDownLatch` with a 180-s safety timeout. `DiagActivity` overrides `onPromptYesNo` to build a non-cancellable `AlertDialog` with two buttons (`diag_dl5_fission_prompt_yes` / `_no`) that invoke the callback. **YES branch** → records `confirmedClusterDisplay = displayId`, extracts the Yandex taskId via a properly-scoped awk pass (`extractYandexTaskIdOnDisplay`: walks the dump, tracks the current `displayId=N` block, and only matches `(mPreferredTopFocusableRootTask|mLastFocusedRootTask)=Task{…A=…yandexmaps}` INSIDE that block — the very awk fix the v1.2.47 false-negative diagnostic was missing), stores it in `yandexTaskIdOnCluster` for F10/F11/F12, marks PASS with « user confirmed cluster on display N (taskId=…) ». **NO branch** → fires `am start --display 0 -n <component>` to reparent Yandex back to the head unit, sleeps 1.2 s, fires `am force-stop` so the next candidate launches from a clean slate, marks PASS with « user said NO — display N is not the cluster ». **TIMEOUT branch** → WARN with « user prompt timed out (180 s) on display N ». Every step logs `prompt title` / `prompt msg` / `user answer` in `r.detail` so the field report captures exactly what the user saw and what they answered (in their locale). **F10** is new: `cmd activity set-task-windowing-mode <taskId> 5` on the confirmed cluster display, the workaround for the display-level fullscreen lock that made v1.2.47's `task resize` silently no-op. SKIPs cleanly if no display was confirmed. **F11** unchanged shape (`cmd activity task resize <taskId> 100 80 1820 640`) but now runs against the user-confirmed display + freeform-locked task. **F12** verifies the new bounds (same `-A 25` task-block scope as v1.2.47). **F13** moves Yandex back to display 0 via the resolved launcher. **F14 / F15 / F16** cleanup: force-stop Yandex, reset the FORCE_RESIZE_APP compat override, then close the projection (`sendInfo 18` + `sendInfo 0`) **only if F01 opened it** — never tears down a projection the user had already started manually. **i18n.** 4 new keys × 12 locales = 48 entries: `diag_dl5_fission_prompt_title`, `diag_dl5_fission_prompt_msg_fmt` (formatted with `%1$d` for the display id, with the standard `\n\n` UX separator between the question and the per-display context), `diag_dl5_fission_prompt_yes`, `diag_dl5_fission_prompt_no`. Hand-curated translations per locale; Uzbek apostrophes properly escaped as `\'`; zero `MissingTranslation` lint regression. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (305→306, 1.2.47-beta→1.2.48-beta), `dilink5/Dl5ClusterReconRunner.java` (Listener gains `default onPromptYesNo(...)`; FissionState gets 4 new fields — `projectionOpenedByUs`, `confirmedClusterDisplay`, `yandexTaskIdOnCluster`, `lastLaunchedDisplay`; new `FISSION_CANDIDATE_DISPLAYS = {2,3,4}` constant; fissionCatalog rewritten with 16 entries; runFissionOne dispatcher rewritten; new step methods `fissionActivateProjection`, `fissionAttemptDisplay(int)`, `fissionPromptDisplay(int, Listener)`, `fissionSetFreeformOnCluster`, `fissionResizeOnCluster`, `fissionVerifyBoundsOnCluster`, `fissionMoveBackToMain`, `fissionCloseProjection`, `extractYandexTaskIdOnDisplay(int)`; old `fissionDetectDisplay` / `fissionLaunch` / `fissionVerifyOnFission` / `fissionResizeTask` / `fissionVerifyBounds` / `fissionMoveToMainDisplay` / `fissionVerifyOnMainDisplay` / `extractTaskMode` / `isFissionName` removed; ~530 LoC net), `DiagActivity.java` (+`onPromptYesNo` override on the fission `Listener` anonymous class, ~17 LoC), `res/values*/strings.xml` × 12 (+4 keys each via a single `multi_replace_string_in_file` batch). No new permission, no manifest change, no new dependency, no new layout, no new drawable. **Zero behavioural regression** on any platform — the change is strictly scoped to the F-tier diagnostic battery. Non-DL5 platforms still see the same single SKIPPED row. The R-tier (R01..R30) catalog and runner are untouched. The R-tier Listener uses the new default `onPromptYesNo` (= NO) and never triggers a prompt because no F-step is in the R-catalog. The `Test Fission` button stays gated to `View.VISIBLE` only on `Platform.get().isAutoDetectedDiLink5()`, with the same confirm dialog as v1.2.41. **Behavioural contract.** DL5 testeur — install v1.2.48 over v1.2.47, open Diagnostic → Cluster DL5 → **Test Fission** → Confirm: F01 opens the cluster projection (the testeur should see the BYD cluster wake up to a black/system surface within ~2 s), F02 resolves the Yandex launcher, F03 enables the compat override, then for each candidate display in {2, 3, 4} F04/F06/F08 launches Yandex there and F05/F07/F09 immediately popups « Yandex Maps est-il visible sur l'écran cluster ? (test sur le display N) » with Oui / Non buttons in the device locale. Saying **Non** routes Yandex back to display 0, kills it, and the next pair Fxx/Fxx+1 starts on the next candidate display. Saying **Oui** records that display as the cluster, skips the remaining candidates (they show SKIPPED with the message « display X already confirmed as cluster »), then F10 sets the task to freeform, F11 resizes it, F12 verifies the new bounds, F13 moves it back to display 0, F14 force-stops it, F15 resets the compat override, F16 closes the projection. Saying **Oui** on no display (or letting all three prompts time out) leaves F10–F12 as SKIPPED with « no cluster display confirmed », while cleanup F13–F16 still runs. The exported report via **Copy report** / **Send via Telegram** keeps the same `renderFissionReport` layout but now contains the user's per-display answers + the confirmed cluster display id + the on-display taskId, so the next diagnostic iteration knows exactly which display id to target. |
| **1.2.47-beta** | 305 | **DL5 Fission runner — fixes after first field run.** Field test on a real DL5 (run `log/byd_report_20260525_142241.log`) scored « 10 PASS / 2 WARN / 0 FAIL » but two of those PASS were false positives that hid a real launch failure : **F05** ran `am start -a MAIN -c LAUNCHER -p ru.yandex.yandexmaps` → `am` printed « Error: Activity not started, unable to resolve Intent » yet still exited 0, so the runner marked PASS. **F09** same intent shape → same false PASS. **F10** verified « `displayId=0` appears anywhere in the dump » which is always true on any device (display 0 is the main head-unit screen). Plus **F06/F08** grep filters were too broad : they matched bounds and windowing modes of unrelated parent/split tasks, never scoped to the Yandex task block. **Fixes (runner-only, zero UI change, zero behavioural change outside the F-tier diagnostic battery).** **F02** now also resolves the actual launcher `ComponentName` via `PackageManager.getLaunchIntentForPackage("ru.yandex.yandexmaps")` (returns `ru.yandex.yandexmaps/.SplashScreen` on the field ROM). The resolved component is stored in `FissionState.targetActivity`. If no launcher activity exists, F03..F10 SKIP cleanly with a precise message. **F05** rewritten as `am start --display N --windowingMode 5 -n <pkg/.Activity>` using the resolved component — bypasses the over-restrictive action/category filter that failed to match Yandex's launcher on this ROM. Stdout is now scanned for « unable to resolve » → mapped to FAIL (and `abortFromHere`) regardless of `am` exit code, so a silent intent-resolution failure can no longer hide behind exit 0. **F09** identical fix : `am start --display 0 -n <pkg/.Activity>` ; « unable to resolve » → WARN (cleanup-adjacent step, doesn't abort cleanup chain). **F06** rewritten with a two-pass dumpsys read : (1) the existing human-readable grep stays as the report detail block, (2) a new awk pass walks `dumpsys activity activities`, tracks the most recent `displayId=N`, and on the first `A=<uid>:ru.yandex.yandexmaps` line prints both `DISP=<displayId>` and `TASK=<full task line>`. The runner then parses `DISP=` as the authoritative per-task display id (no more false positives from substring sweeps) and extracts the windowing mode from the task line's own `mode=...` token via the new `extractTaskMode(taskLine)` helper. **F08** scopes `mBounds=Rect` extraction to `grep -A 25 -E '#<taskId>[^0-9]'` so it can only match lines that belong to the Yandex task block (and the regex `[^0-9]` makes #1499 not match #149). The check now requires BOTH the `(100, 80` origin AND the `1820` right edge on the same line, so a stray `100` from an unrelated config can no longer trigger a false PASS. The « bounds not confirmed » message is also clarified to point out the task is likely not visible / not yet relayouted (resize only takes effect on the next layout pass). **F10** same awk trick as F06 : finds the displayId that immediately precedes the Yandex task line in the dump hierarchy ; PASS iff `parsedDisplayId == 0`, WARN with the actual display id if the move did not take effect (e.g. « task still on display 3 (move not effective) »). Catalog descriptions for F02 / F05 / F09 rewritten to reflect the new logic + the field-test root cause. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (304→305, 1.2.46-beta→1.2.47-beta), `dilink5/Dl5ClusterReconRunner.java` (FissionState +`targetActivity`, F02/F05/F09 implementations rewritten, F06/F10 awk-scoped extraction + helper `extractTaskMode`, F08 task-block scoped grep, ~120 LoC net). No new permission, no manifest change, no new dependency, no new string, no layout change. **Zero behavioural regression.** R-tier recon catalogs (R01..R30) and every other panel (Beta, DL2, DL4, Mirror, Cluster DL5 recon-v2, Voice, Export APK) are untouched — the change is strictly scoped to the F-tier diagnostic battery's 12 test implementations + its shared mutable state. Non-DL5 platforms still see the same single SKIPPED row. **Behavioural contract.** DL5 testeur — install v1.2.47 over v1.2.46, open Diagnostic → Cluster DL5 → **Test Fission** → Confirm : F01 detects the fission display, F02 resolves the Yandex launcher activity (now visible in the F02 row message, e.g. « ru.yandex.yandexmaps installed, launcher=ru.yandex.yandexmaps/.SplashScreen »), F03 enables the FORCE_RESIZE_APP compat override, F04 force-stops Yandex, F05 launches Yandex on the fission display via the resolved component — if the launch fails because the intent can't be resolved, F05 is now FAIL (not PASS) and the rest of F06..F08 are SKIPPED. F06 reports the actual per-task displayId + windowing mode taken from the Yandex task line itself. F07 resizes the task. F08 reports whether the new bounds actually landed in the Yandex task block (not in some unrelated split-screen task). F09 moves the task back to display 0 via the resolved component. F10 confirms the move based on the displayId that immediately precedes the Yandex task in the dump (so if the task is still on display 3, the row says exactly that). F11+F12 cleanup are unchanged. The report rendered by **Copy report** / **Send via Telegram** keeps the same `renderFissionReport` layout but the per-step verdicts are now trustworthy. |
| **1.2.46-beta** | 304 | **Diagnostic — cold-start perf : lazy test-row preparation.** Field report : the Diagnostic page felt slow to open. **Root cause.** `DiagActivity.onCreate()` was eagerly inflating the test-row catalogs of all 6 diag panels — Beta Engine (28 rows) + DiLink 5 (31) + DL5 Cluster recon (42) + DiLink 2 (40) + DiLink 4 (40) + Mirror (10) = **191 row inflations × ~6 `findViewById` per row** on the UI thread, even though only one panel is ever visible at a time. On DL3/DL5 that's ~400–800 ms of bound UI-thread work before the first frame can be drawn. The 5 non-active panels' inflations are pure waste — the typical user opens Diag, looks at one tab, and goes back. **Fix.** Each `prepare<X>TestRows()` is now wrapped behind a lightweight `prepare<X>TestRowsIfNeeded()` guard (one `boolean <x>RowsPrepared` flag per panel) and the call moves out of `onCreate` into `showPanelForTab(position)` so the catalog is only inflated the first time that panel actually becomes visible. Once prepared, the guard is a no-op so subsequent tab switches stay free. The default tab (selected at the end of `onCreate` via `tabs.selectTab` → `showPanelForTab(defaultTab)`) still gets its rows ready before the first frame is drawn — so the user sees exactly the same content as before, just much faster. Pattern is identical to the existing `refreshExportApkListIfNeeded()` and `onVoicePanelEntered()` (which were already lazy since v1.2.42/v1.2.43). **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (303→304, 1.2.45-beta→1.2.46-beta), `DiagActivity.java` (+6 `boolean` fields, +6 `prepare<X>TestRowsIfNeeded` wrappers, removed 6 eager calls from `onCreate`, added 6 lazy calls in `showPanelForTab`). No new permission, no manifest change, no new dependency, no new string, no new layout. **Zero behavioural regression.** Same rows, same bindings, same Run-All / Copy / Telegram pipelines — only the inflation moment changes (`onCreate` → first `showPanelForTab` for that tab). The default tab logic (`isAutoDetectedDiLink2 → TAB_DILINK2`, then DL4, then DL5, then Beta as fallback) is unchanged ; whichever tab opens first, its catalog is prepared synchronously inside `showPanelForTab` before the panel becomes visible. **Behavioural contract.** Any platform — install v1.2.46 over v1.2.45, open Diagnostic : the page opens noticeably faster (one catalog inflated instead of six). Switching to another tab inflates that catalog the first time and is instant on every subsequent switch. Every existing test, button, report and Telegram action keeps the exact same behaviour as v1.2.45. |
| **1.2.45-beta** | 303 | **Main — new « Mode compact (liste apps étroite) » Settings toggle : freed left column gives the cluster preview pane significantly more room.** Field request: a user reported wanting to shrink the apps column on the home screen to ~2 icons wide so the cluster preview on the right pane could grow (both in width and height) and use the screen real estate more efficiently. The user also explicitly asked for it to be a Settings switch so each driver can pick the layout they prefer rather than forcing a single behaviour. **New Settings pref.** `SettingsActivity.PREF_COMPACT_APPS_PANEL = "compact_apps_panel"` (default `false` → historical 5-column layout preserved, zero regression for users who don't enable it). Toggle row injected in `activity_settings.xml` right under the existing « Filtres par catégorie » row in the « Affichage » card, with the standard `ic_aspect_ratio` icon, headline `settings_compact_apps_panel` and support text `settings_compact_apps_panel_support`. Wired in `SettingsActivity` with the same pattern as every other boolean toggle (bindView → loadPreferences → setOnCheckedChangeListener → `getSharedPreferences(PREFS_NAME).edit().putBoolean(...).apply()` + `AppLogger.i`). **Layout switch in MainActivity.** New helper `applyCompactAppsPanelMode()` that reads the pref and applies three idempotent changes : (a) the left apps column's `layout_weight` flips between `1.4` (normal) and `0.6` (compact), achieved by mutating `llAppListSection.getLayoutParams()` on the existing `LinearLayout.LayoutParams` instance — no view re-inflation, no allocation per call ; (b) the `rv_apps` `GridLayoutManager` spanCount flips between `5` (normal) and `2` (compact), but only when currently in grid mode (list mode has no spanCount, so it's left untouched and still works fine in the narrower column) — guarded by `getSpanCount()` comparison so the LayoutManager is only re-created when actually needed ; (c) the filter chips row (`ll_category_filters`) is force-hidden in compact mode because the chip row no longer fits in a 2-icon column ; toggling compact OFF restores chips visibility per their own `PREF_SHOW_CATEGORY_FILTERS` pref so both prefs remain independent. **Live apply.** `applyCompactAppsPanelMode()` is called from `MainActivity.onResume()` (right after the existing `refreshNavHotspot()`), so the new layout kicks in as soon as the user comes back from Settings — no activity restart, no rebind dance, no flicker on cold start (`onResume` runs synchronously after `onCreate` before the first frame is drawn, so the initial column weight + spanCount are correct from the very first paint). **toggleViewMode() updated.** The list↔grid button in the search bar already re-creates the `GridLayoutManager` on every toggle ; the new `nv` branch now reads the compact pref to pick the right spanCount (`compact ? 2 : 5`), so toggling list→grid while in compact mode keeps the 2-column layout. **i18n.** 2 new keys × 12 locales = 24 entries. New keys : `settings_compact_apps_panel` (toggle headline), `settings_compact_apps_panel_support` (sublabel explaining « Libère de l'espace pour le preview cluster — 2 colonnes au lieu de 5 »). French (default) shipped manually with the proper UX phrasing ; 11 other locales (`en`, `de`, `es`, `it`, `tr`, `ru`, `uk`, `be`, `kk`, `uz`, `ar`) injected via the idempotent script `/tmp/i18n_v1_2_45.py` with hand-curated translations per locale (apostrophes properly escaped as `\'`, technical identifiers kept untouched). Zero `MissingTranslation` lint regression. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (302→303, 1.2.44-beta→1.2.45-beta), `SettingsActivity.java` (+`PREF_COMPACT_APPS_PANEL` constant, +`cbCompactAppsPanel` field, +bindView/loadPreferences/listener wiring, ~25 LoC), `activity_settings.xml` (+1 toggle row in Display card), `MainActivity.java` (+`applyCompactAppsPanelMode()` method ~50 LoC, +1 call in `onResume`, `toggleViewMode()` updated to honour the spanCount), `res/values/strings.xml` + 11 other locales (+2 keys each). No new permission, no manifest change, no new dependency, no new drawable (reuses existing `ic_aspect_ratio`). **Zero behavioural regression.** The pref defaults to `false`, so every user who upgrades to v1.2.45 without touching the new Settings toggle sees the exact same layout as v1.2.44. Users who toggle it ON get the compact layout instantly on return from Settings ; toggling it OFF restores the 1.4 weight + 5-col grid + the chips visibility per their own pref. **Behavioural contract.** Any platform — install v1.2.45 over v1.2.44, open Settings → Affichage → enable « Mode compact (liste apps étroite) » : the apps column narrows to a 2-icon grid, the cluster preview pane on the right expands to fill the freed horizontal space, and any visible chip row is hidden so the column stays clean. Disable the toggle → layout reverts to the historical 5-col grid + 1.4 weight + chip visibility per its own pref, immediately on return to Main. List↔grid view toggle (search bar button) keeps honouring compact mode through subsequent flips. |
| **1.2.44-beta** | 302 | **Hotspot — UX consistency pass : navrail unified across all secondary screens, uptime chrono now real-time, connected-clients enumeration fixed.** Four bugs reported from the field, all fixed in this release. **Bug 1 — navrail inconsistent between secondary screens.** Until now the « Hotspot » entry only existed on the Main activity navrail ; entering Diag / Sysinfo / Log / Settings made it visually disappear, breaking the user's mental model of « the navrail is always the same set of icons ». Fix : new helper `NavRailHotspot.apply(activity, viewId, finishOnNav)` in `com.byd.dashcast.NavRailHotspot` that replicates the existing runtime gating logic (DL3 + `use_own_sim` pref defaulting to true since v1.2.38) and wires the click handler. Each of the 4 secondary layouts (`activity_diag.xml`, `activity_log.xml`, `activity_sysinfo.xml`, `activity_settings.xml`) receives a new `LinearLayout` wrapper (id `nav_hotspot_diag` / `_log` / `_sys` / `_set`, `visibility="gone"` by default — the helper flips it to VISIBLE only when the gating passes), pinned just below the bottom weight spacer so it visually mirrors the position used on Main. The 4 activities each call `NavRailHotspot.apply(this, R.id.nav_hotspot_<suffix>, true)` from their existing `wire<Screen>NavRail()` method — single line of glue per screen. **Bug 2 — navrail missing entirely from Hotspot activity.** Entering Hotspot dropped the user into a screen with only a back button in the top bar, no navrail at all, forcing a finish-and-relaunch to reach any other screen. Fix : `activity_hotspot.xml` root changed from a single `NestedScrollView` to a horizontal `LinearLayout` containing : (a) a full navrail on the left (96 dp, Apps/Settings/Diag/Sysinfo/Log entries clickable + Hotspot entry marked active with `bg_nav_pill_active` and primary tint, matching the highlight style used on Log/Settings), (b) the original 2-col content wrapped in a `NestedScrollView` with weight=1. The original top bar (back button + title) is preserved unchanged inside the scroll view. `HotspotActivity` gets a new `wireHotspotNavRail()` method called from `onCreate` that wires the 5 other navrail items as `startActivity + finish` (same pattern used by Diag / Log / Sysinfo / Settings). The Hotspot entry itself is purely decorative (no click — we're already on Hotspot). **Bug 3 — uptime counter not refreshed in real time.** The session uptime chrono was only updated when the `statsTick` runnable fired, which happens every `STATS_PERIOD_MS` = 5 s (the ADB-backed presence probe period). The counter visibly jumped 5 seconds at a time, making it look frozen between updates. Fix : a new independent ticker `uptimeTick` posted on its own `uptimeHandler` at `UPTIME_TICK_MS` = 1 s, which only updates `tv_stat_uptime` from the locally-tracked `upStartElapsed` reference. It fires no ADB call, no `dumpsys`, no `TrafficStats` read — pure `SystemClock.elapsedRealtime()` subtraction + `formatUptime()`, ~µs per tick. Lifecycle : started in `onResume`, removed in `onPause`. The 5 s `statsTick` is unchanged and still drives Rx/Tx + clients + the DOWN→UP session lifecycle. **Bug 4 — connected clients not shown.** The previous `parseClients` relied exclusively on `dumpsys wifip2p` to enumerate peers, walking lines flagged as `"Client:"` / `"mClients:"` / `"WifiP2pDevice"`. On the BYD ROM this section is empty (or formatted differently) even when a device is actually connected to the TetherFi GO, so the user saw « 0 clients » with a phone clearly visible in TetherFi's own UI. Fix : the enumeration now combines **three independent data sources** in a single ADB roundtrip (`dumpsys wifip2p ; echo ===ARP=== ; cat /proc/net/arp ; echo ===NEIGH=== ; ip neigh show`) and takes the **union** of MACs discovered. **Source 1 — dumpsys wifip2p** (unchanged primary path, kept for the device-friendly-name harvest). **Source 2 — /proc/net/arp** : every entry whose `Device` column starts with `p2p-`, `p2p`, `ap`, contains `ap0`/`softap`, or ends in `-ap`, with `Flags != 0x0` (i.e. a complete entry, not an incomplete probe), is treated as authoritative — if a peer has an IP on our tether interface, it IS connected. **Source 3 — `ip neigh show`** : same logic for kernels that surface neighbours only via the `ip` tool and not via `/proc/net/arp` (some recent Android variants). Lines containing `FAILED` / `INCOMPLETE` are dropped. The three sources merge into a `LinkedHashMap<mac, HClient>` (insertion-ordered dedup), so the dumpsys-derived `deviceName` wins when available and the ARP/neigh fallbacks fill in the rest. Now every device with an IP on our hotspot is visible — even when the WifiP2pGroup dump section is empty. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (301→302, 1.2.43-beta→1.2.44-beta), new `NavRailHotspot.java` (~50 LoC), `HotspotActivity.java` (+`wireHotspotNavRail`, +`uptimeTick` + `uptimeHandler` + `UPTIME_TICK_MS`, rewritten `parseClients` + `refreshClients`), `DiagActivity.java` / `LogActivity.java` / `SysInfoActivity.java` / `SettingsActivity.java` (+1 line each calling the helper), `activity_diag.xml` / `activity_log.xml` / `activity_sysinfo.xml` / `activity_settings.xml` (+1 hotspot wrapper each), `activity_hotspot.xml` (root rewrapped + navrail prepended). No new dependency, no new permission, no new string, no manifest change. **Zero behavioural regression** on non-DL3 platforms : the new navrail entries stay `gone` (helper bails out when `Platform.isDiLink3()` is false), so DL4/DL5 users see the exact same navrail as before. **Behavioural contract.** On a DL3 with the « I use my own SIM » toggle on (default since v1.2.38), every navrail in the app shows the same 6 entries (Apps / Settings / Diag / Sysinfo / Log / Hotspot), with the active screen highlighted. Entering Hotspot now keeps the navrail visible for one-tap escape to any other screen. The session uptime visibly ticks every second. The clients card shows every device connected to the TetherFi hotspot (MAC + IP, friendly Wi-Fi Direct name when available). |
| **1.2.43-beta** | 301 | **Diagnostic — new « Voice (alpha) » tab : step 1/3 of the cross-DiLink voice-control roadmap (audio-chain validator, fully isolated, zero ML, zero touch to production code).** Roadmap : v1.2.43 = chaîne audio + VU-meter, v1.2.44 = wake word « Ok DashCast » via openWakeWord (ONNX Runtime Mobile, FOSS Apache-2.0), v1.2.45 = ASR multilangue via Vosk avec packs téléchargés à la demande. La séparation 3 paliers est volontaire : on prouve d'abord que le micro arrive jusqu'à l'app sur DL3/DL4/DL5 *avant* de gonfler l'APK avec les dépendances ML. **Stack choisi** (et pourquoi pas Porcupine) : Porcupine impose un AccessKey nominatif par utilisateur final + validation en ligne périodique + free tier limité à ~3 monthly-active-users + binaire propriétaire, donc inadapté à un APK distribué hors-ligne ; openWakeWord + Vosk + ONNX Runtime Mobile sont 100 % FOSS, sans clé, sans réseau, fonctionnent sur tous les DiLink ≥ 3 sans Google Play Services. **Architecture v1.2.43 (isolée).** Tout le code voice vit dans `com.byd.dashcast.voice.*`, aucun import vers le reste de l'app à part `AppLogger` (logging) et `R` (strings de la notification). Production code zero-touch : aucun fichier de prod n'est modifié sémantiquement — `DiagActivity` reçoit juste un nouvel onglet en append, `AndroidManifest` reçoit une nouvelle permission + un nouveau `<service>`, `activity_diag.xml` reçoit un TabItem + un `<include>`. Le service peut être tué à n'importe quel moment sans impact sur le reste. **New tab.** `activity_diag.xml` index 13 (appended after Export APK BYD), visible sur toutes les plateformes — la décision de support cible est « DL3/DL4/DL5 only » mais la discrimination se fait à l'exécution (feature detection mic) plutôt qu'au manifest, donc minSdk reste à 28. **Layout** `include_diag_voice.xml` (~190 LoC, NestedScrollView root) : header MaterialCardView (`ic_btn_speak_now` tinté primary + titre + pill d'état + sous-titre + ligne sample rate + bouton toggle tonal **Démarrer l'écoute** / **Arrêter** + chrono mm:ss), VU-meter MaterialCardView (deux `ProgressBar` horizontaux 0..32767 : RMS moyen tinté primary, pic instantané tinté tertiary, ligne stats monospace « RMS X • pic Y • clip Z • frames N »), hint final spelling out the no-data-recorded / 16 kHz mono 16-bit / wake word and ASR coming in v1.2.44 + v1.2.45 contract. **VoiceService** (`com.byd.dashcast.voice.VoiceService`, ~280 LoC) : `Service` foreground avec `foregroundServiceType="microphone"` (requis sur API 30+ pour lire AudioRecord en background, DL5 = API 32), notification persistante channel `dashcast_voice_poc` IMPORTANCE_LOW, ouvre `AudioRecord(MediaRecorder.AudioSource.VOICE_RECOGNITION, 16_000, CHANNEL_IN_MONO, ENCODING_PCM_16BIT, max(minBuf, FRAME_SAMPLES*2*4))`. Thread `voice-capture` priorité normale lit par frames de 800 samples (≈50 ms), calcule sumSq + peak + clip count en pure boucle Java (aucune lib native, aucune allocation par frame), publie via `LocalBroadcastManager` sur `ACTION_LEVEL` (extras `EXTRA_RMS`, `EXTRA_PEAK`, `EXTRA_CLIP`, `EXTRA_FRAMES`, `EXTRA_RUN_MS`) au plus toutes les 50 ms pour throttler l'UI. Transitions d'état publiées sur `ACTION_STATE` (`STATE_STARTED` / `STATE_STOPPED` / `STATE_ERROR` + `EXTRA_REASON`). Toute erreur AudioRecord (`getMinBufferSize` ≤0, ctor exception, state ≠ INITIALIZED, `startRecording` exception, `read` ≤0) → `broadcastError` + `stopSelf` propre. `stopCapture` symétrique : `mRunning=false` → join thread (timeout 500 ms) → `mRecord.stop()` → release → broadcast STOPPED. Singleton statique `sIsRunning` pour que l'UI reflète le bon état au moment du rebind (orientation change ou re-entrée dans l'onglet). **Permissions.** Nouvelle `RECORD_AUDIO` dans le manifest, demandée à *runtime* via `ActivityCompat.requestPermissions` au premier clic sur Démarrer (request code `RC_VOICE_RECORD_AUDIO = 0x7AC0`). Refus → toast localisé `diag_voice_perm_denied` + bouton repasse à « Démarrer ». Service déclaré `android:exported="false"` + `foregroundServiceType="microphone"`. Aucune autre permission ajoutée. **DiagActivity wiring.** Nouveau `TAB_VOICE = 13`, `TAB_COUNT` 13→14. Nouveaux fields : `panelVoice`, `tvVoiceStatePill`, `tvVoiceSampleRate`, `tvVoiceRunTime`, `tvVoiceStats`, `pbVoiceRms`, `pbVoicePeak`, `btnVoiceToggle`, `voiceReceiverRegistered`, `voicePanelBound`, `voiceReceiver` (BroadcastReceiver privé final qui dispatche `ACTION_LEVEL` et `ACTION_STATE`). Nouvelles méthodes : `bindVoicePanel`, `onVoicePanelEntered`, `registerVoiceReceiverIfNeeded`, `unregisterVoiceReceiver`, `onVoiceToggleClicked`, `startVoiceService`, `applyVoiceState`, statique `formatVoiceRunTime`, override `onRequestPermissionsResult`. `showPanelForTab` étendue avec `isVoice` + visibility gate + `onVoicePanelEntered()` à l'entrée. `onDestroy` étendu avec `unregisterVoiceReceiver()` pour ne pas leaker le receiver. `onCreate` étendu avec `bindVoicePanel()`. Aucune modification sémantique des autres branches. **i18n 100 % — 19 nouvelles clés × 12 locales = 228 entrées.** Nouvelles clés : `diag_tab_voice`, `diag_voice_header_title`, `_header_subtitle`, `_btn_start`, `_btn_stop`, `_state_idle`, `_state_running`, `_state_error_fmt`, `_perm_denied`, `_meter_label`, `_meter_rms`, `_meter_peak`, `_stats_fmt` (`RMS %1$d • pic %2$d • clip %3$d • frames %4$d`), `_stats_idle`, `_hint` (contrat no-data-recorded + roadmap v1.2.44 + v1.2.45), `_sample_rate_fmt`, `_notif_channel`, `_notif_title`, `_notif_text`. Français (default) + anglais shippés à la main avec la phraséologie UX correcte ; les 10 autres locales (`ar`, `be`, `de`, `es`, `it`, `kk`, `ru`, `tr`, `uk`, `uz`) injectées via le script idempotent `/tmp/i18n_v1_2_43.py` avec traductions hand-curated par locale (identificateurs techniques `DashCast` / `openWakeWord` / `Vosk` / `RMS` / `VOICE_RECOGNITION` / `kHz` / `mono` / `16-bit` / `Ok DashCast` / `PoC` / `ML` / `clip` / `frame` laissés tels quels, placeholders `%1$d` / `%2$d` / `%3$d` / `%4$d` / `%1$s` strictement préservés). Zéro régression lint `MissingTranslation`. **Nouvelle dépendance.** `androidx.localbroadcastmanager:localbroadcastmanager:1.0.0` ajoutée — broadcast in-process uniquement, aucune IPC système, aucune perm supplémentaire. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (300→301, 1.2.42-beta→1.2.43-beta, +1 dep), `AndroidManifest.xml` (+`RECORD_AUDIO`, +`<service>` voice), nouveau `voice/VoiceService.java`, nouveau `res/layout/include_diag_voice.xml`, `res/layout/activity_diag.xml` (+1 TabItem + 1 `<include>`), `DiagActivity.java` (+~150 LoC : tab constant, fields, receiver, bind/start/stop pipeline, permission handler, onDestroy cleanup), `res/values/strings.xml` + `res/values-en/strings.xml` (+19 clés chacun), 10 autres locales (+19 clés chacune via le script i18n). Zéro autre fichier modifié. **Zero behavioural regression.** Aucun chemin de prod (cluster, freedom, overdrive, mirror, sniffer, export apk…) n'est touché. Le service ne démarre jamais sans clic utilisateur explicite. Le receiver UI n'est enregistré qu'à l'entrée dans l'onglet Voice (lazy). Si le user n'ouvre jamais l'onglet, aucun bytecode voice n'est exécuté (à part le static init de la classe). APK +~40 KB seulement (un service + un layout + 19 strings × 12 locales). **Behavioural contract.** Toute plateforme — install v1.2.43 par-dessus v1.2.42, ouvrir Diagnostic → Voice (alpha), cliquer **Démarrer l'écoute** : popup système RECORD_AUDIO → Autoriser → notification persistante « DashCast — écoute micro (PoC) » apparaît → pill passe à « En écoute… » → bouton devient « Arrêter » → les deux ProgressBar bougent en temps réel selon le bruit ambiant → ligne stats égrène RMS/pic/clip/frames → chrono mm:ss avance. Cliquer **Arrêter** ou quitter l'activité → service stoppé, notification retirée, ProgressBar à zéro, pill à « Service arrêté ». Si AudioRecord échoue (mic indisponible sur la ROM), pill affiche « Erreur : <message> » et la cause exacte est dans `AppLogger` (tag `VoiceService`) — c'est exactement ce qu'on cherche à mesurer sur les 3 plateformes cibles avant d'investir dans openWakeWord. |
| **1.2.42-beta** | 300 | **Diagnostic — new « Export APK BYD » tab : list every BYD/XDJA/DiLink/cluster package and ship its APK to Telegram in one tap, with auto-cleanup.** Field need: when investigating an unknown BYD component (smarttravel, commander, freedom, overdrive, window_mgmt, containerservice, clusterdebug…) the only reliable way to RE its behaviour is to grab the actual `.apk` from the production ROM — which until now required a USB cable, `adb pull /data/app/.../base.apk`, a host machine, and someone to do the dance in person. The new tab does it from inside the car in two taps. **New tab.** `activity_diag.xml` index 12 (appended after Cluster DL5), default visible on every platform (the listing is informational and the export action is harmless). New layout `include_diag_export_apk.xml` (~125 LoC, NestedScrollView root): header MaterialCardView (`ic_share` icon tinted primary + title + subtitle + counter line « X APK détectés • Y MB sur disque » + tonal **Rafraîchir** button); a hint TextView spelling out the cache+share+auto-delete contract; programmatic list container `ll_export_apk_list`. **Row layout** `row_export_apk.xml` (~95 LoC) — visually mirrors `row_log.xml` (4 dp colored bar on the left, `bg_log_row_info` rounded background, monospace typography) so the user immediately recognises the « one line per item » convention from the Journal screen: package name (primary tint, bold), `v<versionName> (<versionCode>)`, file size (right-aligned), APK path on a second line with `ellipsize=middle` to keep `/data/app/~~hash~~/base.apk` readable, and a tonal **Exporter** button at the end. **Scanner.** `scanBydPackages()` runs `PackageManager.getInstalledPackages(0)` off the UI thread, filters by case-insensitive substring match on `com.byd.` / `com.xdja.` / `com.dilink.` / `cluster` / `automotive` / `fission` / `projection` / `dilink` (covers every known BYD-side package family on DL2/DL3/DL4/DL5), reads `applicationInfo.sourceDir` to compute the on-disk size, sorts alphabetically. Lazy: triggered only when the tab is first opened (`refreshExportApkListIfNeeded` from `showPanelForTab`) and rerun on every **Rafraîchir** click. **Export pipeline.** Click on a row's **Exporter** button → background thread → reads the APK from `sourceDir` (every APK in `/data/app/` is mode `0644` so the app uid can read it directly via `FileInputStream`, no ADB shell needed — equivalent of `adb pull` minus the USB cable) → copies it to `getExternalFilesDir(null)/exported_apks/<pkg>-v<vername>-<vercode>.apk` (versionName sanitised through `sanitiseVer` to strip any non-alphanumeric char that could break the filename) → posts back to UI → fires `Intent.ACTION_SEND` with MIME `application/vnd.android.package-archive` + `FileProvider`-wrapped `content://` URI (`com.byd.dashcast.fileprovider` is already declared for the existing log/report sharing) + `FLAG_GRANT_READ_URI_PERMISSION` → walks 5 known Telegram packages (`org.telegram.messenger`, `org.telegram.messenger.web`, `org.telegram.plus`, `nekox.messenger`, `org.thunderdog.challegram`), targets the first one installed via `setPackage(hit)` so the chooser is bypassed → falls back to the generic chooser if no Telegram variant is found or if the direct intent throws. **Auto-cleanup contract** (user requirement: « il faut qu'une fois l'APK envoyé, on garde pas le fichier stocké dans la mémoire de la voiture »). Three independent layers : (1) `wipeExportApkCacheDir()` runs eagerly when `bindExportApkPanel` is called during `onCreate` — wipes every orphaned APK from a previous session before the user even sees the tab. (2) Same wipe re-runs on every **Rafraîchir** click, so the cache never grows beyond what the user is actively exporting. (3) `scheduleExportApkDelete(file)` registers a `Handler.postDelayed(..., 5 minutes)` on the main looper after the share intent is fired, deleting the cached APK after 5 minutes — long enough for Telegram to finish uploading even on a slow connection, short enough that the file never lingers. The handler is `removeCallbacksAndMessages(null)` in `onDestroy` so a destroyed activity does not leak its scheduled deletes (also obeys the standard sanity-check rule for Handler.postDelayed lifecycle). Net result: even in the worst case (user exports an APK, Telegram is missing, chooser is dismissed without sharing), the file is wiped on the next open of the tab. **DiagActivity wiring.** New constants `TAB_EXPORT_APK = 12`, `TAB_COUNT = 13`. New fields: `panelExportApk`, `tvExportApkCounters`, `btnExportApkRefresh`, `llExportApkList`, `exportApkInitialised`, `exportApkCleanupHandler`, the static nested `ExportApkEntry` type, plus the constants `EXPORT_APK_SUBDIR` / `EXPORT_APK_AUTO_DELETE_MS` (= 5 min) / `EXPORT_APK_NEEDLES`. New methods : `bindExportApkPanel`, `refreshExportApkListIfNeeded`, `refreshExportApkList`, `scanBydPackages`, `onExportApkClicked`, `shareApkToTelegram`, `scheduleExportApkDelete`, `exportApkCacheDir`, `wipeExportApkCacheDir`, plus three private statics (`copyFile`, `sanitiseVer`, `formatBytes`). `showPanelForTab` extended to handle the new tab and call `refreshExportApkListIfNeeded` on first visit. `onDestroy` extended with `exportApkCleanupHandler.removeCallbacksAndMessages(null)`. **i18n 100% — 15 new keys × 12 locales = 180 entries.** New keys: `diag_tab_export_apk`, `diag_export_apk_header_title`, `_header_subtitle`, `_refresh`, `_counters_idle`, `_counters_scanning`, `_counters_fmt` (`%1$d APK • %2$s on disk`), `_hint` (explains the cache+share+auto-delete contract), `_btn_export`, `_btn_exporting`, `_version_fmt` (`v%1$s (%2$d)`), `_sent_fmt`, `_chooser_title`, `_err_fmt`, `_err_no_source`. French (default) + English shipped manually with the proper UX phrasing; the other 10 locales (`ar`, `be`, `de`, `es`, `it`, `kk`, `ru`, `tr`, `uk`, `uz`) injected via the idempotent script `/tmp/i18n_v1_2_42.py` with hand-curated translations per locale, respecting the established invariants (no machine pseudo-translation, technical identifiers `APK` / `BYD` / `XDJA` / `DiLink` / `cluster` / `Telegram` left untouched, format placeholders `%1$s` / `%2$d` strictly preserved). Zero `MissingTranslation` lint regression. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (299→300, 1.2.41-beta→1.2.42-beta), `DiagActivity.java` (+~225 LoC net : tab constant, fields, bindings, scan/render/export/cleanup pipeline, onDestroy handler cleanup), new `res/layout/include_diag_export_apk.xml`, new `res/layout/row_export_apk.xml`, `res/layout/activity_diag.xml` (+1 TabItem + 1 `<include>` between Cluster DL5 and Coming Soon), `res/values/strings.xml` + `res/values-en/strings.xml` (+15 keys each, scoped to the new panel — no rename of any existing key), 10 other locales (+15 keys each via the i18n script). No new permission, no new manifest entry (the existing `FileProvider` `com.byd.dashcast.fileprovider` covers `getExternalFilesDir` via the already-declared `<external-files-path name="logs" path="."/>` wildcard), no new dependency, no new drawable, no new style. **Zero behavioural regression** on any platform — the tab is purely additive, scanning is lazy, no shell command is ever dispatched from the new code path, no system mutation is performed, and the cleanup contract guarantees no APK is ever persisted on the car's storage. **Behavioural contract.** Any platform — install v1.2.43 over v1.2.42, open Diagnostic → Export APK BYD: the tab lists every BYD-side package installed on the ROM with its version + size + source path. Pressing **Exporter** on any row copies the APK to a temporary cache, opens Telegram (or the chooser if Telegram is not installed) pre-populated with the APK file ready to send, surfaces a « APK envoyé : <pkg> » toast, then transparently auto-deletes the cached copy 5 minutes later. Pressing **Rafraîchir** wipes the cache immediately and re-runs the scan. Closing and reopening the app, or just switching tab and coming back, always shows the same authoritative list with no stale exports. |
| **1.2.41-beta** | 299 | **DL5 Cluster diag — recon cleanup (R14–R17) + new Test Fission live battery (F01–F10) targeting Yandex Maps.** The v1.2.40-beta DL5 field report (`log/byd_report_20260525_113903.log`, 27 PASS / 2 WARN / 0 FAIL / 1 SKIPPED) confirmed three things needed for the next step: (a) `ChangeId(174042936; name=FORCE_RESIZE_APP; disabled; overridable)` is registered and overridable on the DL5 ROM, (b) Yandex Maps declares `resizeMode=RESIZE_MODE_UNRESIZEABLE` at the activity level so it is the canonical blocker we want to bypass, and (c) the global `force_resizable_activities` flag is still `0` (reverted in v1.2.35), so we must use the per-package compat override rather than re-flipping the system-wide knob. It also surfaced two recon bugs we fix here. **Bug — R14/R15/R16 wrong AOSP syntax.** v1.2.38-beta wrote them as `am compat list <package>` for read-back, but on the DL5 production ROM the first argument of `am compat list` is interpreted as a **change ID**, not a package name (every call returned `Unknown or invalid change: 'com.byd.dashcast'` / `'ru.yandex.yandexmaps'` / `'com.waze'`). The proper per-package override read-back is `dumpsys platform_compat`, not `am compat list`. **Fix.** R14 is rewritten as `am compat 2>&1 \| head -20` (clean usage dump on stderr-merged stdout, no stack trace, surfaces every supported verb on this ROM). R15 becomes `dumpsys platform_compat 2>&1 \| sed -n '/PackageOverrides:/,/^$/p' \| head -60` — lists every currently-set per-package compat override on the device, which is the data we actually want before flipping FORCE_RESIZE_APP on Yandex. R16 becomes `dumpsys platform_compat 2>&1 \| grep -E 'ru\\.yandex\\.yandexmaps\|com\\.waze\|com\\.byd\\.dashcast' \| head -20` — pre-flight check that our three test packages have no leftover override that could skew the next F-battery run. **Bug — R17 BusyBox grep BRE alternation.** v1.2.40-beta wrote `grep -B1 -A1 '174042936\\\|FORCE_RESIZE_APP'` (BRE alternation), but the BusyBox grep on the DL5 ROM does not honor the BRE `\\|` — R17 returned 0 lines on the testeur's device, even though the same pattern with `-E` (in R18) matched correctly. **Fix.** Switched to `grep -E -B1 -A1 '174042936\|FORCE_RESIZE_APP'` (ERE alternation, BusyBox-compatible). Catalog descriptions for R14–R17 were rewritten end-to-end with an inline note explaining the v1.2.40→v1.2.41 transition. **New feature — `Test Fission` live test battery (F01–F10, DL5 only).** Recon-only suites are not enough: the user explicitly asked for « une batterie de nouveaux tests pour essayer de faire en sorte d'avoir le redimensionnement et le move qui fonctionne sur le cluster en ciblant Yandex Maps comme client de test ». New dedicated tertiary-style **Test Fission** button in `include_diag_cluster_dl5.xml`, in a separate action row below the existing Run-recon / Copy / Telegram row, gated to `View.VISIBLE` only when `Platform.get().isAutoDetectedDiLink5()` (hidden on every other platform — DL2/DL3/DL4 never see the button). Confirm dialog before launch (the test is destructive: it kills Yandex Maps, mutates a compat override, then cleans up). The same row container `ll_dl5_cluster_test_list` and renderer (`bindDl5Row`) are reused — the row prep is refactored into a shared `prepareClusterDl5TestRowsFor(List<TestDef>)` that either takes the R-catalog or the F-catalog, with a `dl5ClusterShowingFission` flag steering the Copy / Send Telegram exporters toward the right renderer (`renderReport` for R-tier, `renderFissionReport` for F-tier). **F-battery (12 tests, ~30 s typical):** **F01** detect fission display via `DisplayManager.getDisplays()`, prefer the lowest non-zero id with a `fission|xdja|cluster` name match, fall back to any non-zero id. **HARD RULE: id=0 is explicitly excluded as a fission target — if no non-zero display is available, the whole F-tier SKIPs without executing any shell command.** **F02** verify Yandex Maps is installed (`PackageManager.getPackageInfo`). SKIPs F03..F12 cleanly if absent. **F03** `am compat enable 174042936 ru.yandex.yandexmaps 2>&1` — enables FORCE_RESIZE_APP on the target. **F04** `am force-stop ru.yandex.yandexmaps` — the compat override only takes effect on the next `Application.onCreate`, so the live process has to be killed first. **F05** `am start --display <fissionId> --windowingMode 5 -a android.intent.action.MAIN -c android.intent.category.LAUNCHER -p ru.yandex.yandexmaps 2>&1` — launches Yandex on the fission display in `freeform` windowing mode. Hard guard at this step: `fissionId <= 0` short-circuits to FAIL + sets `abortFromHere`. **F06** waits ~3 s, then `dumpsys activity activities` filtered to `ru\.yandex\.yandexmaps|displayId=|mWindowingMode=|Task=Task|mBounds=Rect`. Parses the first `#\\d+` token on any line containing the package name to extract the taskId, and asserts (a) `displayId=<fissionId>` and (b) `mWindowingMode=freeform` (or `windowingMode=5`) appear in the filtered dump. **F07** `cmd activity task resize <taskId> 100 80 1820 640 2>&1; echo __exit=$?` — applies a 100×80 inset rectangle inside the 1920×720 fission framebuffer. PASS = `__exit=0`. **F08** waits ~2 s, then dumpsys filtered to `#<taskId> |mBounds=Rect|mWindowingMode=`, asserts the new bounds are present. **F09 (NEW)** `am start --display 0 -a android.intent.action.MAIN -c android.intent.category.LAUNCHER -p ru.yandex.yandexmaps 2>&1` — moves the Yandex task back to the main display (id 0). User clarification: the « don't touch display 0 » rule means « no resize / no size-mutation command on display 0 », not « never address display 0 at all » — moving an app TO display 0 via the standard launcher intent is the exact same code path the system fires when the user taps the app icon on the head unit, so it's explicitly safe and validates the move pipeline end-to-end (fission → main). **F10 (NEW)** waits ~2 s, dumpsys filtered to `displayId=|Task=Task|ru\.yandex\.yandexmaps`, asserts `displayId=0` is present on a line that also mentions the package. **F11** cleanup `am force-stop ru.yandex.yandexmaps` — leaves the device empty of Yandex state, ready for a fresh test on a different fission display. **F12** cleanup `am compat reset 174042936 ru.yandex.yandexmaps 2>&1` — restores Yandex Maps to its declared resize behaviour, leaves zero state behind for the next test or for the user's normal Yandex usage. F11 + F12 are cleanup steps that run **unconditionally** (even if F03..F10 aborted) so the device is always left clean between runs. **Hard safety rules baked into the runner.** (1) DL5-only enforcement: `runFission` checks `Platform.get().isAutoDetectedDiLink5()` before any work — on non-DL5 platforms every F-result is marked SKIPPED with the message `DL5 only — current platform: <describeMode(ctx)>` and no shell command is dispatched. (2) Display 0 policy clarified by user: « pas de commande qui change la taille » sur display 0 (no resize / no `wm size` / no `cmd activity task resize` / no `am display set-overscan`) — but moving an app TO display 0 via the standard launcher intent (F09) is explicitly allowed since it's the exact same code path the system uses when the user taps the app icon. F01 still excludes display 0 from the **fission target** candidate list (we only fission-launch on id > 0), F05/F07/F08 still gate every per-display command behind `st.fissionDisplayId > 0`, and F09 uses the safe MAIN/LAUNCHER intent — no resize command is ever issued against display 0. (3) The compat override mutation kills only Yandex Maps (the test target), never DashCast itself — the v1.2.40 R17 self-kill class of bug cannot recur with this design because the change ID is paired with `ru.yandex.yandexmaps`, not with `com.byd.dashcast`. (4) F-tier results are exported via a dedicated `renderFissionReport` (separate header, separate target/compat-id summary) so the report is self-contained and disambiguous from the R-tier recon report. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (298→299, 1.2.40-beta→1.2.41-beta), `dilink5/Dl5ClusterReconRunner.java` (R14–R17 dispatcher + catalog rewrites; new `fissionCatalog()` + `emptyFissionResults()` + `runFission(Context, Listener)` + `runFissionOne(...)` + 10 F-step implementations + `FissionState` + `renderFissionReport(...)`, ~340 LoC net), `res/layout/include_diag_cluster_dl5.xml` (existing action row preserved, new vertical `ll_dl5_cluster_fission_row` block added below it containing the tertiary-style `btn_dl5_cluster_test_fission` + a hint TextView), `DiagActivity.java` (+`btnDl5ClusterTestFission` / `llDl5ClusterFissionRow` / `dl5ClusterShowingFission` fields, +`prepareClusterDl5TestRowsFor(List<TestDef>)` refactor, +`confirmAndRunClusterDl5FissionTests` + `runClusterDl5FissionTests`, copy/Telegram exporters now switch renderer based on `dl5ClusterShowingFission`), `values/strings.xml` + `values-en/strings.xml` (+6 new keys: `diag_dl5_cluster_test_fission`, `_hint`, `_confirm_title`, `_confirm_msg`, `_confirm_ok`, `_only_dl5`; full 12-locale i18n pass — the same 6 keys translated and shipped in `values-ar`, `values-be`, `values-de`, `values-es`, `values-it`, `values-kk`, `values-ru`, `values-tr`, `values-uk`, `values-uz` via the idempotent script `/tmp/i18n_v1_2_41.py`, total +60 entries — zero `MissingTranslation` lint regression). No new permission, no manifest change, no new dependency, no protocol bump. **Behavioural contract.** DL5 testeur — install v1.2.41-beta over v1.2.40, open Diagnostic → Cluster DL5: the existing **Run recon** / **Copy report** / **Send via Telegram** trio is unchanged, with a new violet **Test Fission** button + warning hint appearing on a second action row. Pressing **Run recon** still runs the 30-test R-catalog (now with R14–R17 properly returning data instead of error noise). Pressing **Test Fission** brings up a confirm dialog explaining the destructive nature; on confirmation the F-catalog replaces the row list, F01 detects the fission display, F02–F10 execute sequentially with live row updates, and at the end the testeur can press **Copy report** or **Send via Telegram** to ship the F-tier report back. DL2 / DL3 / DL4: the **Test Fission** row is `View.GONE`, every existing path is unchanged. **Zero behavioural regression** on any platform — the change is purely additive on the UI side and the runner's existing `runAll`/`renderReport` paths are untouched. |
| **1.2.40-beta** | 298 | **Critical recon bug fix: R17 was killing the DashCast process mid-suite.** DL5 tester running v1.2.39-beta recon-v2 reported: « à un moment l'écran flash et ça sort de DashCast pour revenir. Du coup le résultat est perdu et la personne ne peut pas exporter le log. » Root cause: R17 was implemented in v1.2.38-beta as `am compat reset 174042936 com.byd.dashcast 2>&1` — a deliberate idempotent probe against our own package, expected to be a no-op when no override was set. The flaw: **Android 11+ platform_compat service forces a full process restart of the target package on EVERY compat-override mutation (including a reset that resolves to a no-op)**. This is by design — compat changes are evaluated at `Application.onCreate`, so the platform has to recycle the process to ensure the new value takes effect on the next launch. Net effect on DL5: the recon suite called `am compat reset` against its own package, the platform killed DashCast's process, DiagActivity was recreated when the user re-opened the app, the in-memory `dl5ClusterLastResults` list was wiped, and the tester was left with no report to copy or send via Telegram. **Fix.** R17 is now a fully read-only probe: `dumpsys platform_compat 2>&1 \| grep -B1 -A1 '174042936\|FORCE_RESIZE_APP' \| head -30` — surfaces the same registered metadata of change ID 174042936 (registered? overridable? default state? min sdk?) without touching the override state of any package, hence zero process kill and zero side effect on any other app. The catalog description for R17 was rewritten to reflect the change and to document the v1.2.38→v1.2.40 regression history inline, so the report itself is self-documenting for future testers. **No other test in the catalog mutates state** — R13 (`am compat enable` no args), R14–R16 (`am compat list <pkg>` read-back), R18 (`dumpsys platform_compat | grep`) are all read-only, and R01–R12 / R19–R30 are pure dumpsys / settings get / pm dump / getprop / service list / wm size queries. So R17 was the ONLY suite-killing test. After this fix, the suite runs to completion every time and the report can be copied / shared via Telegram normally. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (297→298, 1.2.39-beta→1.2.40-beta), `dilink5/Dl5ClusterReconRunner.java` (R17 dispatcher in `runOne` + R17 description in `catalog()`). No new permission, no string change, no layout change, no new dependency, no protocol bump. **Zero behavioural regression** on every other platform — the recon tab is DL5-only-targeted and the change is a pure command swap inside a single test case. |
 **Two field-reported UX fixes: (a) Hotspot icon now visible by default on DiLink 3 navrail, (b) projection no longer auto-starts when launching the DashCast app.** **Fix A — Hotspot navrail icon missing on DL3.** A DL3 user reported the Hotspot icon in the left navigation rail never appeared on their device. Root cause: the `refreshNavHotspot()` gate added previously (in a forward-tagged comment dated "v1.2.42") read `getBoolean(PREF_USE_OWN_SIM, false)` — defaulting to `false` meant new DL3 users had to discover and tick the "I use my own SIM card" toggle in Settings before the icon appeared, which most users never did. Fix: default now flipped to `true`, so on DL3 the Hotspot navrail entry appears immediately on first launch (out-of-the-box discoverability of the TetherFi feature shipped in v1.2.36-beta). Users without a personal SIM can still hide the icon via the same Settings toggle (the checkbox and its support string are unchanged). The gate stays in place — only the default is flipped — so the runtime path is identical (DL3 + opted-in → VISIBLE + click handler; everything else → GONE). **Fix B — Auto-projection on app launch removed.** Since v1.2.77 (well before the current beta channel — the comment is a forward-tag and the behaviour landed long ago in fact), opening the DashCast app would unconditionally call `activateCluster()` in `onStart()` if no cluster service was running. User feedback: "I don't want projection to start automatically when I open the app. It's enough that projection auto-starts when I launch an app on the cluster." Rationale acknowledged: every cluster-bound use of DashCast goes through tapping an app in the launcher grid, and `onSendToDashboard()` already auto-activates the cluster (slow path) when `mClusterService == null` or `getDisplayId() <= 0`, then replays the tapped app via `mPendingAppAfterActivation` once the service is up (fast path). So the launch-time `activateCluster()` call was redundant for any actual workflow, and intrusive for users opening DashCast just to browse the app grid, tweak Settings, view SysInfo, or trigger an OTA update — none of which require the cluster surface to be powered on. Fix: the `else { activateCluster(); }` branch in `MainActivity.onStart()` is removed; the preceding `if (ClusterService.sIsRunning)` branch is preserved untouched so an already-running service still gets re-bound when the Activity returns to foreground. **Slow path** = `onSendToDashboard()` → `mClusterService == null` → `mPendingAppAfterActivation = app ; activateCluster()` then the service-connected callback replays the app once `displayId > 0`. **Fast path** = `onSendToDashboard()` → service already bound, `displayId > 0` → direct `moveTaskToDisplay()` (or `launchOnDashboard()` for cold app). Both paths are unchanged and explicitly documented in the inline comment that replaces the removed branch. **Behavioural contract.** Opening DashCast cold (no projection active) now lands on the app grid with the cluster status pill in "OFF" state and no shell calls fired — exactly like opening any standard launcher. Tapping any app in the grid still kicks the slow path automatically (`mPendingAppAfterActivation` is set, `activateCluster()` runs, and the tapped app is replayed once the cluster comes up — same flow as v1.2.83+). Opening DashCast while projection is already running (`ClusterService.sIsRunning == true`) still binds and re-attaches the listener — no regression on the "leave then come back" scenario. **Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (296→297, 1.2.38-beta→1.2.39-beta), `MainActivity.java` (`refreshNavHotspot` default flip from `false` to `true` + explanatory comment; `onStart()` else-branch removed + replaced with comment documenting the slow/fast paths preserved in `onSendToDashboard`). No new permission, no string change, no layout change, no new dependency, no protocol bump. **Zero behavioural regression on DL2/DL4/DL5** — `refreshNavHotspot` is still platform-gated on `isDiLink3` (the icon stays GONE on every non-DL3 platform regardless of the pref value), and the `onStart` change applies uniformly to every platform (the v1.2.77 auto-activation behaviour was global, so its removal is global too — but it was never desirable on any platform per the user's clarification). |
 **Two field-reported bug fixes: (a) UpdateChecker infinite-prompt loop on v1.2.37-beta installs, (b) recon-v2 — 8 bug fixes in the DL5 Cluster Recon tab, including hidden display id=2 detection.** **Bug A — UpdateChecker loop.** Multiple v1.2.37-beta users reported the OTA banner re-proposing v1.2.37-beta install on every launch, even after the install completed. Root cause traced in `UpdateChecker.isNewer(String latest, String currentName, int currentCode)`: the helper strips the `-beta` / `-buildN` suffix from `latest` via `stripSuffix(...)` before calling `parseVer(...)`, but does NOT apply the same treatment to `currentName`. So `parseVer("1.2.37-beta")` calls `String.split("\\.")` → `["1","2","37-beta"]`, then `Integer.parseInt("37-beta")` throws `NumberFormatException` which is caught and silently leaves that segment at 0. Result: `current` parses as `[1,2,0]` while latest parses as `[1,2,37]`, the version-segment comparison loop returns `lv > cv = 37 > 0 = true` at index 2 → `isNewer` returns `true` → OTA prompt shown → user installs → install completes → next launch → same loop. Fix is a one-liner: apply `stripSuffix` to `currentName` before parseVer, mirroring exactly what's done for `latest`. Also tightened the post-loop equality path: when both base versions are identical and no `-buildN` suffix is present (`latestBuild < 0`), the helper now explicitly returns `false` (`latestBuild > 0 && latestBuild > currentCode`) instead of falling through. So v1.2.37-beta installed users will, on first launch with v1.2.38-beta, see one final correct update prompt, install v1.2.38-beta, and then never be re-prompted for the same version again. v1.2.36-beta and earlier users are unaffected. **Bug B — Recon-v2.** Field log `log/byd_report_20260525_105105.log` from the DL5 tester (Yandex Maps on cluster) running the v1.2.37-beta recon-v1 tab revealed 8 distinct bugs in `Dl5ClusterReconRunner.java`, all fixed in this release. (1) **R01 NPE.** `probePlatform` called `Platform.get().describeMode(null)` — the `null` argument crashes on `ctx.getApplicationContext()` inside the `describeMode` implementation. Fixed: `probePlatform(Context ctx, TestResult r)` now takes a Context and passes it through. (2) **R02 last-match bug.** `probeDisplays` walked `DisplayManager.getDisplays()` and overwrote `cluster = d.getDisplayId()` on every name match without breaking — so when DL5 exposes both `shared_fission_bg_XDJAScreenProjection_0` (id=3) and `_1` (id=4), the loop ended on id=4 instead of the (slightly more canonical) lowest matching id. Fixed: explicit `break` after the first match. (3) **R02 / R25 hidden display.** DL5 (and possibly other DL platforms) hides the physical cluster display from `DisplayManager.getDisplays()` for non-system uids — the dumpsys headers reveal a `Display Id=2` that is completely invisible to the framework call. v1.2.37 recon-v1 missed this entirely (only DL Manager APIs were probed). Fixed: `probeDisplays` now combines (a) the `DisplayManager.getDisplays()` enumeration with (b) a `dumpsys display | grep 'Display Id='` fallback that lists every display id the platform_compat / SurfaceFlinger layer sees, including hidden ones. The new R25 (`probeClusterDisplayGeometry`) was rebuilt around the same dumpsys parse: it extracts every `Display Id=N` value via `grep -oE`, deduplicates with `sort -u`, then probes `wm size -d N` + `wm density -d N` for every non-zero id found (not just the heuristic "name contains fission/xdja/cluster/virtual" match). This catches the geometry of id=2 too, which is the actual cluster framebuffer. (4) **R03 token mismatch.** The PASS check required token `"uid=2000"` in stdout but `id -u` outputs only `"2000"` — so even on a perfectly working ADB shell, R03 reported WARN. Fixed: required tokens changed to `"2000"` and `"shell"` (Android `id -un` returns `"shell"` for uid 2000). (5–8) **R13–R18 wrong AOSP syntax.** The recon-v1 catalog assumed `am compat get <change>` was a valid verb (it's in some out-of-tree AOSP forks). On the DL5 production ROM the verb is rejected with `Invalid toggle value: 'get'` and `am compat --help` is also rejected with `Argument expected after compat`. The DL5 platform_compat shell exposes ONLY `enable | disable | reset | reset-all | list`, and `list` requires a package argument. Fixed: **R13** now probes `am compat enable 2>&1 | head -40` (no package → triggers the official usage hint, non-destructive); **R14** is now `am compat list <ourPackage> 2>&1 | head -80` (proper read-back syntax against our own package); **R15** is `am compat list ru.yandex.yandexmaps 2>&1 | head -80` (the actual target of the FORCE_RESIZE_APP fix); **R16** is `am compat list com.waze 2>&1 | head -80`; **R17** is `am compat reset 174042936 <ourPackage> 2>&1` (idempotent reset of the change ID against ourselves — confirms 174042936 is registered, no-op when no override is set); **R18** is `dumpsys platform_compat 2>&1 | grep -E 'FORCE_RESIZE_APP\|OVERRIDE_MIN_ASPECT_RATIO\|NEVER_SANDBOX_DISPLAY_APIS' \| head -20` (final cross-check that the symbolic change names are registered in the platform_compat service). The catalog descriptions in `catalog()` were rewritten to match the new commands and to explicitly document the AOSP verb surface as discovered on DL5. (9) **R23 grep noise.** The recon-v1 grep `'Stack\|RootTask\|displayId\|mWindowingMode'` matched the `mGlobalConfig` / `mOverrideConfig` / `CurrentConfiguration` spam in `dumpsys activity activities` (every Configuration carries these field names too) → 30 lines of noise, zero useful task info. Fixed: grep now reads `grep -v mGlobalConfig | grep -E '^  Display #|Task=Task|mBounds=Rect|mWindowingMode=|displayId='` — the `-v` pre-filter strips the global-config block, the second grep is anchored on the actual task-stanza prefixes. (10) **R26–R28 wrong attribute.** The grep was `'resizeable\|maxAspectRatio\|launchMode'` which matched the `<supports-screens>` block's `resizeable` attribute (manifest-level, declares the app supports being resized **by the launcher across screen sizes**) instead of `<activity android:resizeableActivity>` (activity-level, declares the activity can be put in freeform / multi-window).
