| **1.2.13-build204** | 204 | **DL5 — per-app resize finally works: `findRunningTaskId` falls back to `dumpsys activity recents` via the proxy daemon (fixes "sliders move but Yandex window doesn't resize"), and `MainActivity` stops sending the dead `wm overscan` command on DL5.** Field log of DL5 testeur (`log/BYD_RE_Sniffer_20260523_141144.txt`, 15.3 MB, post-v1.2.12 install) showed the user moving the W/H sliders repeatedly and tapping Apply (40+ `I BYDApp: Applied custom resize NNN/NNN for ru.yandex.yandexmaps` lines spread over 14:12:16–14:13:14) with two consecutive symptoms in the daemon log: (a) every Apply emits `D AdbLocalClient: executeShellWithResult: wm overscan W,H,W,H -d 3 -> Unknown command: overscan` — the `wm overscan` subcommand was removed from Android in API 30+ and DL5 ships API 32, so the shell returns "Unknown command" every single time; (b) **ZERO `ClusterService: findRunningTaskId ru.yandex.yandexmaps → taskId=…` lines** AND zero `resizeActiveTask … OK` lines — meaning the typed-verb `IActivityTaskManager.resizeTask(taskId, bounds, RESIZE_MODE_FORCED)` path is never invoked. Root cause for (b): `ClusterService.findRunningTaskId` uses `ActivityManager.getRunningTasks(50)`, which on API 21+ returns **only the caller's own task** for non-system apps (Android lockdown of cross-package task introspection) — DashCast is not system → the loop never finds Yandex → returns -1 → `resizeActiveTask(-1, …)` early-returns at `if (taskId <= 0) return;` SILENTLY (no log line, hence the user-visible "nothing happens"). **Display 3 vs display 2 subtlety (re-confirmed, no action needed).** The cluster app's task lives on `mDisplayId=3` (1920×1080 shadow framebuffer managed by `containerservice`), while the visible composed face is on `displayId=2` (1920×720). For touch (v1.2.7) we had to call `MotionEvent.setDisplayId(2)` so the InputDispatcher routes events from the composed face to the underlying task. For resize, no such trick is needed: `resizeTask(taskId, bounds)` operates in the coord system of the task's **current** display (i.e. display 3, 1920×1080) and the XDJA fission compositor handles the projection/scaling to display 2 at composition time. The bounds computation in `applyClusterFreeformBounds` already uses `Display.getRealSize()` for the task's display (display 3 → `1920×1080`, confirmed by `I ClusterService: cluster FREEFORM bounds=Rect(215, 164 - 1705, 916) display=3 1920×1080` in the same log). The only thing missing was a valid taskId. **(1) Fix (b) — `findRunningTaskId` adds a daemon-shell fallback.** Path 1 (kept for legacy ROMs / DL3 where DashCast may still have GET_TASKS): `ActivityManager.getRunningTasks(50)` enumerated, returns the matching `t.id` if any. Path 2 (new on v1.2.13, triggered when path 1 returns nothing or only the caller's own task): if `BetaProxyClient.isConnected()`, call `BetaProxyClient.runShell("dumpsys activity recents")` (the proxy daemon runs uid 2000 = shell, has SHELL perms → dumpsys returns the full recent-tasks table cross-package) then parse with a fast-path regex `Task\\{[^}]*#(\\d+)[^}]*\\bA=<pkg>\\b` (matches the standard AOSP line `* Recent #0: Task{xxxxxxx #88 type=standard A=ru.yandex.yandexmaps U=0 ...`) and a fallback block-split scanner that accepts `realActivity=<pkg>/...` or `cmp=<pkg>/...` within the same `* Recent #N:` block (covers BYD ROM variants where the affinity field is rewritten). Returns the first match (most-recent task) or -1. The block parser is a static helper `parseTaskIdFromDumpsysRecents(String dump, String pkg)` so it's unit-testable in isolation. Every path emits a discrete log line (`(via AM)`, `(via daemon dumpsys recents)`, `not found in dumpsys recents (out.length=N)`, `daemon not connected; cannot fallback`) so future field logs immediately show which branch was hit. **(2) `resizeActiveTask` no longer silent on taskId<=0.** The early-return `if (taskId <= 0) return;` now emits `W ClusterService: resizeActiveTask: taskId<=0 for pkg=<pkg> — cannot resize (lookup via AM + daemon dumpsys both failed)` so a residual failure surfaces in the log instead of vanishing. **(3) Fix (a) — `MainActivity` skips `wm overscan` on DL5.** Both `btnResizeApply.onClick` and `autoApplyInsetsIfNeeded` now wrap the `wm overscan` shell with `if (AdbLocalClient.isDiLink5Safe(this)) { AppLogger.d(TAG, "… DL5: skipping wm overscan (cmd removed in API 30+) — resizeTask handles it"); }`. Mirrors the existing skip in `ClusterService.applyClusterFreeformBounds` (which had already been gated in v1.2.8 but `MainActivity` was overlooked). On DL3/DL2 the call is unchanged (DL3 still uses `wm overscan -d 1` which is supported on its API 29 ROM). **(4) Behavioural contract.** DL3 Seal EU production: `findRunningTaskId` path 1 succeeds (DashCast has effective GET_TASKS for its own package + sometimes BYD AUTO grants more), path 2 is never reached → byte-for-byte identical behaviour. `MainActivity` resize still sends `wm overscan -d 1` (DL3 API 29 accepts it) and `resizeTask` typed verb (BYD ROM exposes it). DL5 testeur: Apply now finds Yandex's taskId via the daemon dumpsys, `IActivityTaskManager.resizeTask(taskId, Rect(insetH, insetV, 1920-insetH, 1080-insetV), 1)` applies, the XDJA compositor reflects the new bounds on the composed display 2 face → visible resize. DL2: no cluster path, neither code change ever fires. **(5) Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (versionCode 203→204, versionName 1.2.12→1.2.13), `ClusterService.java` (`findRunningTaskId` rewritten to add daemon fallback + per-path logs ~50 LoC, new static `parseTaskIdFromDumpsysRecents(String, String)` helper ~40 LoC, `resizeActiveTask` early-return now logs ~5 LoC), `MainActivity.java` (both `wm overscan` call sites — `btnResizeApply` ~8 LoC and `autoApplyInsetsIfNeeded` ~8 LoC — gated by `isDiLink5Safe`). No new permissions, no new dependencies, no new strings, no protocol bump (still `PROTOCOL_VERSION=2`). **(6) Validation plan.** (a) DL5 testeur — install v1.2.13 over v1.2.12, start cluster projection, launch Yandex Maps on cluster, open the Adjust panel, move W/H sliders, tap Apply. Expected: Yandex window on cluster resizes visibly within the same frame, logcat shows `I BYDApp: Applied custom resize W/H for ru.yandex.yandexmaps` followed by `D ClusterService: findRunningTaskId ru.yandex.yandexmaps → taskId=NN (via daemon dumpsys recents)` followed by `I ClusterService: resizeActiveTask ru.yandex.yandexmaps Rect(insetH, insetV - W, H) OK`. No more `Unknown command: overscan` lines (the `wm overscan` shell is skipped). (b) Auto-apply on launch — restart cluster projection, observe that `autoApplyInsetsIfNeeded` (called 500 ms after launch) produces the same successful resizeTask sequence. (c) DL3 Seal EU — install, navigate, resize sliders work exactly as before (path 1 still succeeds). (d) If the daemon is not running (rare edge case), the WARN `findRunningTaskId … daemon not connected; cannot fallback to dumpsys` surfaces in the log and resize quietly fails with the new `taskId<=0` WARN — both diagnostics visible, no crash. (e) DL2 — install, navigate, no cluster path triggered. |
| **1.2.12-build203** | 203 | **DL5 — KeyboardBridge architectural pivot: AccessibilityNodeInfo.performAction(ACTION_SET_TEXT) instead of KeyEvent injection (fixes v1.2.11 "keyboard pops up but text never reaches the cluster app").** Field log of DL5 testeur (`log/BYD_RE_Sniffer_20260523_140358.txt`, 13.8 MB, captured after v1.2.11 install — `"package":"com.byd.dashcast","versionCode":"202"` confirmed in logcat) showed three coupled facts: (1) `KeyboardBridgeActivity` launches successfully at 14:04:13, gains input focus on display 0 (`input_focus … KeyboardBridgeActivity displayId=0`); (2) the system IME (`ru.yandex.androidkeyboard`) appears immediately afterwards and the user actively taps its keys (~30 `input_interaction … 6682c4a InputMethod` lines over 14:04:15–14:04:25); (3) **ZERO `injectKey FIRST OK` traces from `MirrorDaemon`** across the entire 30-minute capture window — proving the KeyEvent injection chain is never exercised. The smoking-gun line one frame after focus: `W InputMethodManager: Ignoring showSoftInput() as view=android.widget.EditText{… VFED..CL. .F....ID 0,0-1,1} is not served.` — the v1.2.11 1×1 EditText fails the IMM's "served view" check (size below threshold + `showSoftInput` posted before `onWindowFocusChanged` fired the InputConnection setup), so no `InputConnection` is established. The IME visually pops up (because `SOFT_INPUT_STATE_ALWAYS_VISIBLE` on the window is independent of the served view), but typed characters land in a null sink. **Second-order finding (cluster window asymmetry).** Even if v1.2.11 had been "served", the original v1.2.8 KeyEvent path was architecturally fragile on DL5: the cluster app's focused window lives on `mDisplayId=3` (1×1 shadow framebuffer where `containerservice` runs the XDJA fission compositor) while the composed face users actually see is on `displayId=2`. The touch path works because v1.2.7 calls `MotionEvent.setDisplayId(2)` AND the InputDispatcher routes from the composed face to the underlying window in the fission topology — but the symmetric `KeyEvent.setDisplayId(2)` route was never empirically validated, and may not work because the IME's `InputConnection` is bound to the served EditText on display 3, not to a global keyboard dispatcher. **(1) Pivot — replace KeyEvent injection with `AccessibilityNodeInfo.performAction(ACTION_SET_TEXT)`.** The a11y subsystem is cross-display by construction (TalkBack uses the same mechanism to read out content from any display) and is immune to per-display IME isolation or fission compositor asymmetry. New static helpers in `ClusterImeWatcherService`: **(a) `setTextOnCluster(CharSequence text)`** — walks `getWindows()`, picks the window whose `getDisplayId() > 0` (cluster, on API 30+ with `flagRetrieveInteractiveWindows`), falls back to any window on API < 30, retrieves `root.findFocus(FOCUS_INPUT)` per-call (no cached node — nodes from a11y are stale-prone, refresh per call), calls `node.performAction(ACTION_SET_TEXT, Bundle{ACTION_ARGUMENT_SET_TEXT_CHARSEQUENCE_VALUE: text})`. Returns boolean success. **(b) `performImeEnterOnCluster()`** — same node-discovery flow, then calls `AccessibilityAction.ACTION_IME_ENTER` (API 30+) or `ACTION_CLICK` fallback (API 28/29). **(c) Static `volatile ClusterImeWatcherService sInstance`** published in `onServiceConnected`, cleared in `onDestroy`, so the bridge activity can invoke the helpers without `bindService`. **(d) `AccessibilityServiceInfo.flags |= FLAG_RETRIEVE_INTERACTIVE_WINDOWS`** added in `onServiceConnected` (mirrored in `accessibility_ime_watcher.xml` as `android:accessibilityFlags="flagRetrieveInteractiveWindows"`) — required so `getWindows()` returns the cluster windows in addition to the head-unit focused one. **(2) `KeyboardBridgeActivity` rewrite.** Window is now **220 dp × 48 dp** (vs 1×1 in v1.2.11) — large enough that the IMM accepts the EditText as a served view, but still transparent + pinned `Gravity.BOTTOM | Gravity.END` so it doesn't obscure any cluster mirror UI. `showSoftInput` is now called from `onWindowFocusChanged(true)` instead of `onCreate.post(…)`, which removes the race that produced the "is not served" warning. The `TextWatcher.onTextChanged` body is reduced to a single line: `ClusterImeWatcherService.setTextOnCluster(s.toString())` — atomic, no per-character diff, no `KeyCharacterMap` translation, no `KeyEvent` sequencing. `OnEditorActionListener` calls `performImeEnterOnCluster()` then clears the local field. Removed: `KeyCharacterMap mKcm`, `CharSequence mLastText`, `boolean mSuppressWatcher`, `forwardKeyCode`, `forwardKeyEvent`, `mapAsciiToKeyCode`, all `ClusterInputForwarder.injectKeyEvent` calls from the bridge. **(3) Daemon path preserved (deprecated by bridge but still callable).** `MirrorDaemon.injectKey` + `sSetDisplayIdKey` reflection (added in v1.2.11) + `ClusterInputForwarder.injectKeyEvent` are NOT removed — they remain available for any future code path that needs raw `KeyEvent` injection (e.g. dedicated keyboard hardware on the cluster), and removing them would invalidate the v1.2.11 instrumentation in the daemon log. The bridge simply no longer calls them. **(4) Behavioural contract.** DL3 Seal EU production: `ClusterImeWatcherService.onAccessibilityEvent` early-returns on `!isDiLink5`, the bridge button is `View.GONE`, neither helper is ever invoked — zero behavioural change. DL5 testeur: user taps the cluster EditText → a11y service detects focus on `displayId > 0`, launches bridge → bridge appears as a 220×48dp invisible window → IMM serves the EditText → IME pops up → user types → each `onTextChanged` calls `setTextOnCluster` which performs `ACTION_SET_TEXT` on the cluster Yandex editable → text appears character-by-character (well, snapshot-by-snapshot) on the cluster Yandex search field → tap IME's Enter → `performImeEnterOnCluster` fires `ACTION_IME_ENTER` → Yandex executes the search. DL2: completely unaffected (a11y service inert, no cluster path). **(5) Why this fixes the symptom.** v1.2.11 failed because no `InputConnection` was established (1×1 EditText not served) → `TextWatcher` never fired. v1.2.12 fixes the served-view bug (220×48dp + `onWindowFocusChanged`-deferred `showSoftInput`) AND replaces the brittle `KeyEvent.setDisplayId(2)` cross-display routing (compositor-asymmetric, never validated) with the standard a11y `ACTION_SET_TEXT` (cross-display-native, used by every screen reader). **(6) Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (versionCode 202→203, versionName 1.2.11→1.2.12), `KeyboardBridgeActivity.java` (`onCreate` simplified to 220×48 window + EditText with `onWindowFocusChanged`-deferred showSoftInput, `TextWatcher` reduced to one-line `setTextOnCluster` call, `OnEditorActionListener` calls `performImeEnterOnCluster`, removed `KeyCharacterMap` + `forwardKeyCode/forwardKeyEvent/mapAsciiToKeyCode` + `ClusterInputForwarder` import — ~410 LoC → 200 LoC), `ime/ClusterImeWatcherService.java` (+`sInstance` static, +`FLAG_RETRIEVE_INTERACTIVE_WINDOWS` in `onServiceConnected`, +`setTextOnCluster(CharSequence)`, +`performImeEnterOnCluster()`, +`findClusterFocusedEditable()` private helper — ~140 LoC added), `res/xml/accessibility_ime_watcher.xml` (+`android:accessibilityFlags="flagRetrieveInteractiveWindows"`). No new permissions, no new dependencies, no new strings, no protocol bump (`PROTOCOL_VERSION=2` unchanged). **(7) Validation plan.** (a) DL5 testeur — install v1.2.12 over v1.2.11 (no uninstall, versionCode bumped). Start cluster projection, launch Yandex Maps on cluster, tap the search field on the cluster (with a11y service enabled). Expected: IME pops up at the bottom of the head unit, NO `Ignoring showSoftInput() … is not served` warning in logcat (the bug from v1.2.11), each typed character causes the Yandex search field on the cluster to update with the cumulative text, tap IME's Enter → Yandex executes the search. (b) Logcat — search for `ClusterImeWatcher` tag, look for `setTextOnCluster ACTION_SET_TEXT refused` WARN: if present, the cluster editable rejected the action (rare, possible if Yandex's EditText is not exposing the `ACTION_SET_TEXT` capability — would require a per-app workaround). Search for `ClusterImeWatcher: findClusterFocusedEditable scan failed` ERROR: indicates a11y window enumeration failed (would require investigating `FLAG_RETRIEVE_INTERACTIVE_WINDOWS` propagation on this ROM). (c) DL3 Seal EU — install, navigate UI: ⌨ button remains GONE, no log lines from `ClusterImeWatcher` (early-return via `!isDiLink5`). (d) DL2 — install, navigate UI: zero impact. |
| **1.2.11-build202** | 202 | **DL5 — KeyboardBridge: invisible window + KeyEvent routed to cluster display (fixes "text typed on head unit IME doesn't reach cluster app" + removes the French overlay).** Two coupled field bugs reported after v1.2.10 install on DL5 testeur (Yandex Maps search, Russian system locale, screenshot received 23/05/2026): (a) tapping the ⌨ button (or auto-launch via the a11y service) showed an opaque French dialog at the top of the head-unit screen with title `Clavier déporté`, hint `Tapez ici : chaque caractère est envoyé au champ actif du cluster.`, plus three buttons (`Entrée` / `Retour` / `Fermer`) — visually wrong (always FR regardless of system locale, intrusive, covers part of the live cluster mirror), (b) characters typed on the system IME never reached the Yandex search field on the cluster (no character ever appeared in the Russian text input). Root cause analysis: **(a) Visible chrome.** `KeyboardBridgeActivity` was authored in v1.2.8 with a full LinearLayout (title TextView + hint TextView + visible EditText + 3-button row, semi-opaque dark background `0xCC202020`, MATCH_PARENT × WRAP_CONTENT pinned at the top of display 0). The visible chrome was purely cosmetic — the IME pops up regardless of EditText visibility — but the strings were never translated and the overlay was always present. **(b) Cluster doesn't receive keys.** `MirrorDaemon.injectKey(KeyEvent)` called `InputManager.injectInputEvent(kev, ASYNC)` WITHOUT first calling `KeyEvent.setDisplayId(sClusterDisplayId)`. Without the displayId override, the system InputDispatcher routes the key to the **globally focused window**, which on multi-display Android 12 is per-display — but our own `KeyboardBridgeActivity` on display 0 holds the head-unit focus, so it swallows every injected key (the EditText receives them, re-fires the TextWatcher, infinite ping-pong avoided only because TextWatcher diff sees no new chars). Cluster app on display 2 never sees a single KeyEvent. Symmetric pattern to v1.2.7's MotionEvent fix where `MotionEvent.setDisplayId(2)` was added — the touch path worked because of that, the key path was overlooked. **(1) Fix (b) — KeyEvent displayId routing.** `MirrorDaemon`: new static `sSetDisplayIdKey` Method cached via `KeyEvent.class.getDeclaredMethod("setDisplayId", int.class)` alongside the existing `sSetDisplayId` for MotionEvent. `injectKey(KeyEvent)` now calls `sSetDisplayIdKey.invoke(kev, sClusterDisplayId)` (with try/catch inner guard so a reflection failure still attempts the bare inject) BEFORE `InputManager.injectInputEvent`. First-event log line upgraded: `injectKey FIRST OK displayId=N setDisplayIdAvail=true/false keyCode=K action=A` (mirror of the existing motion log). `ClusterInputForwarder`: new instance `mSetDisplayIdKeyMethod` cached in constructor, applied in the direct-fallback path (used when daemon Binder is null). Both paths now route keys to the cluster display id (typically `2` on DL5 — same as touch). **(2) Fix (a) — invisible bridge window.** `KeyboardBridgeActivity.onCreate` rewritten: removed the entire LinearLayout + title + hint + 3-button row. Window is now `1×1` px pinned `BOTTOM | END`, transparent background, dim 0. Sole content view is the focused EditText, stripped of every visible property (`setBackground(null)`, transparent text + hint colours, `setCursorVisible(false)`, zero padding). The IME still pops up automatically thanks to `SOFT_INPUT_STATE_ALWAYS_VISIBLE` + `mImm.showSoftInput` posted on the EditText. The 1×1 footprint means the rest of display 0 (DashCast UI, cluster mirror tile) stays interactive while the bridge is alive — touches outside the 1 px fall through to the windows below. Enter forwarding preserved via the existing `OnEditorActionListener` (IME's Done/Search/Send button → `KEYCODE_ENTER` injected to cluster). Back key naturally closes the activity (Android default). Unused imports (`View`, `Button`, `LinearLayout`) and unused helper (`dp(int)`) removed. The translated strings (`keyboard_bridge_title/hint/btn_enter/btn_back/btn_close/input_hint`) become dead resources — kept in `values/strings.xml` for the activity label in the manifest (`android:label=@string/keyboard_bridge_title`, but `excludeFromRecents=true` so the label is never user-visible). **(3) Behavioural contract.** DL3 Seal EU: bridge is never launched (the ⌨ button is gated `Platform.isDiLink5(this) ? VISIBLE : GONE`, the a11y service short-circuits on `!isDiLink5`) — the new code path is dormant by construction, zero observable change. DL5 testeur: tap the ⌨ button (or focus an EditText on the cluster with the a11y service enabled) → no visible overlay, IME pops up at the bottom of display 0, each typed character forwards to the cluster display via `setDisplayId(clusterId)` + daemon `injectKey` → cluster app's focused EditText receives the keys natively (no app-level integration needed). DL2: completely unaffected (no cluster, ⌨ button hidden, a11y service inert). **(4) Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (versionCode 201→202, versionName 1.2.10→1.2.11), `daemon/MirrorDaemon.java` (+`sSetDisplayIdKey` field, +`KeyEvent.class.getDeclaredMethod("setDisplayId")` in `initInputManager`, +`setDisplayIdKey.invoke(kev, sClusterDisplayId)` in `injectKey` with try/catch inner guard, +upgraded first-event log line — ~15 LoC net), `dashboard/ClusterInputForwarder.java` (+`mSetDisplayIdKeyMethod` field, cached in ctor, applied in direct-fallback path — ~10 LoC), `KeyboardBridgeActivity.java` (entire `onCreate` body replaced ~70 LoC → ~50 LoC, all visible chrome stripped, imports cleaned, `dp(int)` helper removed). No new permissions, no new dependencies, no new strings/layouts, no protocol bump (`PROTOCOL_VERSION=2` unchanged). **(5) Validation plan.** (a) DL5 testeur — install v1.2.11 over v1.2.10 (no uninstall, versionCode bumped). Start cluster projection, launch Yandex Maps on the cluster, tap the search field on the cluster (with a11y service enabled) OR tap the ⌨ button manually. Expected: NO French overlay at the top, only the IME keyboard at the bottom of display 0; type any text → characters appear immediately in the Yandex search field on the cluster; tap IME's Enter (or its hardware equivalent) → KEYCODE_ENTER reaches Yandex (triggers search). (b) Logcat — search for `MirrorDaemon` tag, look for `injectKey FIRST OK displayId=2 setDisplayIdAvail=true keyCode=29 action=0` (the first character typed): this is the smoking-gun that the displayId override took effect. If `setDisplayIdAvail=false` → reflection failed on this ROM, falls back to default routing (cluster won't receive). (c) DL3 Seal EU — install, navigate UI: ⌨ button must remain GONE, no log lines from `KeyboardBridge`, `ClusterImeWatcherService` post-`!isDiLink5` lines absent. (d) DL2 — install, navigate UI: zero impact (no cluster path triggered). |
| **1.2.10-build201** | 201 | **DL5 IME a11y banner — one-click activation via daemon shell + translations for all 11 locales (en, de, es, it, ar, be, kk, ru, tr, uk, uz).** Field report (23/05/2026, post-v1.2.9 install on DL5 testeur in non-FR locale): (a) the IME accessibility onboarding banner introduced in v1.2.8 was hardcoded in French only — every non-FR locale fell back to FR strings; (b) the « Activer » button appeared to do nothing when tapped multiple times. Root cause analysis: **(a) Missing translations.** The 8 IME-related string keys (`a11y_ime_watcher_label/summary/description` + `ime_banner_title/body/btn_enable/btn_later/btn_dismiss/toast_enabled`) only existed in `values/strings.xml` (default = French) — none of the 11 sibling locale folders (`values-en`, `values-de`, `values-es`, `values-it`, `values-ar`, `values-be`, `values-kk`, `values-ru`, `values-tr`, `values-uk`, `values-uz`) had these keys, so Android's resource resolver fell back to the default FR strings on any non-FR system locale. **(b) Enable button silently failing.** The button fired `Intent(Settings.ACTION_ACCESSIBILITY_SETTINGS)` then `startActivity(i)` wrapped in a `Throwable` catch that only logged the error to AppLogger — user saw no visual feedback whatsoever. On BYD AUTO ROM the standard AOSP `ACTION_ACCESSIBILITY_SETTINGS` activity may not be advertised (BYD ships a heavily customised Settings.apk on Android 10/12) so the intent silently failed to resolve, the `Throwable` was caught, the AppLogger line went unnoticed, and nothing visible happened on tap. **(1) Fix (a) — `enableImeA11yServiceOneClick(card, btnEnable)` — direct activation via shell.** The button now skips the Settings UI entirely and uses the proxy daemon's shell (uid=2000, same uid as `adb shell`) to write directly to `Settings.Secure.ENABLED_ACCESSIBILITY_SERVICES` via the `settings` binary. Single-line POSIX `sh -c` script: read current colon-separated service list → if empty or `null` write our component standalone → if already contains our component leave unchanged → else append `:` + our component → write back via `settings put secure enabled_accessibility_services` → flip `settings put secure accessibility_enabled 1` master flag → `echo OUT=$(settings get secure enabled_accessibility_services)` for verification. Posted through `ShellGateway.execShellWithResult` (already serialises to either the proxy daemon or the legacy `AdbLocalClient` fallback chain). On `onSuccess`: re-read the OS state via `ClusterImeWatcherService.isEnabled(this)` (trust the OS, not the shell echo), if `true` → toast `R.string.ime_banner_toast_enabled` + hide banner card; if shell returned OK but the OS still reports disabled (rare, possible if `settings` exists but `accessibility_enabled` is gated by a SELinux policy on this ROM) → fall back to the Settings UI chain. On `onError` (no daemon, no legacy fallback, ROM blocks `settings put`) → also fall back to the Settings UI chain. **(2) Fix (b) — `openA11ySettingsFallback()` — three-tier Settings opener.** When the shell route fails, try three intents in sequence, each wrapped in its own `Throwable` catch: (i) standard `Settings.ACTION_ACCESSIBILITY_SETTINGS` (works on stock AOSP), (ii) direct ComponentName `com.android.settings/.Settings$AccessibilitySettingsActivity` (BYD ROM may not advertise the standard action but still ship the activity), (iii) generic `Settings.ACTION_SETTINGS` (last resort, user manually navigates to Accessibility). If all three fail → toast `R.string.ime_banner_toast_cannot_open_settings` (« Cannot open Settings — please enable the accessibility service manually ») so the user finally gets visible feedback. Button is re-enabled on every code path so a failure doesn't leave it stuck disabled. **(3) Translations.** Added the 10 IME banner string keys (8 original + 1 new `ime_banner_toast_cannot_open_settings` + the FR default also gained the new toast key) to ALL 11 locale folders. Each translation hand-crafted with proper grammar, locale-specific keyboard / cluster terminology, and escaped apostrophes (`\'`). Locales covered: English, German, Spanish, Italian, Russian, Ukrainian, Belarusian, Turkish, Arabic, Kazakh, Uzbek. **(4) Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (versionCode 200→201, versionName 1.2.9→1.2.10), `MainActivity.java` (replace Enable click handler ~5 LoC, new `enableImeA11yServiceOneClick` ~70 LoC, new `openA11ySettingsFallback` ~40 LoC), `res/values/strings.xml` (+1 new key `ime_banner_toast_cannot_open_settings`), `res/values-{en,de,es,it,ar,be,kk,ru,tr,uk,uz}/strings.xml` (+10 keys each = 110 total). No new permissions, no new dependencies, no protocol bump (still `PROTOCOL_VERSION=2`), no new layouts, no behavioural change on DL3 / DL2 (banner is `Platform.isDiLink5`-gated). **(5) Behavioural contract.** DL3 Seal EU production: banner is never shown (`isDiLink5=false`), the new shell helper code path is never invoked, the new fallback chain is never invoked — zero behavioural change. DL5 testeur: tap « Enable/Activate/Aktivieren/Aktiva/Attiva/etc. » → if proxy daemon is reachable (post-bootstrap) → service is enabled in <500 ms with no Settings trip required, banner disappears, toast confirms; if daemon down → falls back transparently to the Settings UI. DL2: completely unaffected (no cluster, no DL5 gate). **(6) Validation plan.** (a) DL5 testeur — install v1.2.10 → switch system locale to English (or German / Spanish / etc.) → confirm banner shows in selected language. (b) Tap the Enable button (whichever localised label) → expected: toast « Auto-keyboard active ✓ » (localised) appears within ~500 ms and banner disappears. Go to Settings → Accessibility → confirm « Cluster auto-keyboard (DashCast) » is checked. (c) Open any cluster app with a text field, tap the field → KeyboardBridgeActivity should now auto-launch on the head unit (validates that the a11y service activation actually worked end-to-end). (d) If the test environment has the proxy daemon disabled, the Enable button should still work — falling back to opening the Settings UI; user manually toggles, returns to MainActivity, and `refreshImeA11yBanner` (already called in onStart) shows the toast and hides the card. (e) DL3 — install, confirm banner never shows. (f) DL2 — same, banner never shows. |
| **1.2.9-build200** | 200 | **DL3/DL5 bug-fixes — long-press Kill no longer leaves a ghost Recents tile, and Stop Projection no longer relaunches the cluster app fullscreen on the head unit (best-effort patch ; the robust fix via typed verb `removeTaskByPackage` is roadmapped for v1.3.x in `docs/PLAN_v1.3.x_FULL_PROXY.md` §7.b).** Field report (23/05/2026, DL3 Seal EU production, also expected on DL5) surfaced two distinct symptoms sharing the same root cause — BYD AUTO ROM's `am force-stop` / `IActivityManager.forceStopPackage` kill the **process** but **do not** remove the `TaskRecord` from the Recents stack, and the existing dumpsys-based cleanup fallback (`dumpsys activity recents | grep | sed → am task remove → TaskRemover app_process`) silently fails on this ROM (uid 2000 lacks `REMOVE_TASKS`, `am task remove` not always available). **(Bug 1 — long-press Kill leaves a ghost Recents tile.)** Long-press on a cluster-active app → `Kill` → process dies BUT the Recents tile remains and tapping it relaunches the activity. Root cause #1: in `MainActivity.doKillApp`, the move-back-to-display-0 (`mClusterService.moveTaskToDisplay(pkg, 0, null)`) and the subsequent `AdbLocalClient.forceStopApp(...)` were fired on different threads in close succession — `forceStopApp`'s internal `dumpsys activity recents | grep | sed` could parse the recents stack DURING the move, see a stale or already-moved TaskId, and `am task remove` would target the wrong (or no longer existing) task. **Fix (a) — serialize move→forceStop via `LaunchCallback`.** `mClusterService.moveTaskToDisplay(pkg, 0, callback)` already supports `LaunchCallback.onResult(boolean)` fired on the main thread (added in v1.1.x). `doKillApp` now extracts the `forceStopApp` callback as a final `killCallback` local, then in the cluster-app branch wraps the move with a `LaunchCallback` that fires `mSessionClusterPackages.remove + persistSessionClusterPackages + AdbLocalClient.forceStopApp(this, pkg, killCallback)` inside `onResult` — guaranteeing the dumpsys-recents scan inside `forceStopApp` runs AFTER the move-to-display-0 has fully completed. The non-cluster branch (else) is unchanged shape but routes through the same extracted `killCallback`. **(Bug 2 — Stop Projection relaunches the cluster app fullscreen on the head unit.)** Stop Projection while Waze (or any cluster app) is active → cluster restored OK, but ~1 s later Waze pops up fullscreen on display 0 (head unit) covering the BYD launcher. Root cause #2: in `MainActivity.restoreBydDashboard` (TEST 10 path) and `originCluster`, the call order was `moveSessionAppsToMainDisplay() → AdbLocalClient.restoreBydOnCluster(...)`. The first call brings every session cluster-launched app (including the active `capturedClusterPkg`) to display 0 as a foreground task. Then `restoreBydOnCluster` force-stops `capturedClusterPkg` — kills the process, but the TaskRecord on display 0 survives. Then `sendInfo(18)+sendInfo(0)` triggers the Qt cluster surface restoration which appears to fire a `RESUME_TOP_ACTIVITY` ripple effect on display 0 that picks up the orphan TaskRecord and resurrects the activity. **Fix (b) — invert order : remove `capturedClusterPkg` from the session set BEFORE `moveSessionAppsToMainDisplay()`.** Now `moveSessionAppsToMainDisplay()` iterates a set that no longer contains the active cluster pkg — it stays on display 1 (cluster). `restoreBydOnCluster` (unchanged) then force-stops it IN PLACE on display 1 just before `sendInfo(18)`. No orphan TaskRecord lands on display 0, no resurrection path. Other session apps (split-mode side dashboards) are still moved to display 0 as before. Same surgical edit applied to `originCluster` (handles the original-cluster restore path used when the user hasn't explicitly chosen "BYD cluster"). **Fix (c) — `verifyForceStop` active escalation (defense in depth, AdbLocalClient).** v1.2.8's `verifyForceStop` only logged a WARN if `BetaProxyClient.getPidsByPackage(pkg)` reported surviving PIDs after the typed `IActivityManager.forceStopPackage` returned ok. Build 200 actively escalates: surviving PIDs → `BetaProxyClient.runShell("kill -9 <pid1 pid2 ...>")` via the daemon (uid 2000 owns the same uid as the target app so the kill is permitted), sleep 200 ms, re-query PIDs, log the final state. The whole escalation is wrapped in an inner try/catch so a daemon glitch never breaks the teardown sequence. Also benefits `restoreBydOnCluster` / `restoreOriginCluster` (typed paths call `verifyForceStop`) — so if the cluster pkg somehow survives the in-place force-stop in Fix (b), the kill -9 escalation finishes the job before `sendInfo(18)` fires. **(Limit / roadmap.)** This v1.2.9 patch fixes the **process-survival** and **race-window** sides of both bugs but does **not** purge the `TaskRecord` from Recents (architectural limitation of the dumpsys+shell pipeline on BYD AUTO ROM). On DL3 the ghost Recents tile in Bug 1 may still persist visually after Kill (tapping it would now relaunch a fresh instance instead of resurrecting state) — but Bug 2's user-visible symptom (Waze fullscreen on the head unit) is fully resolved by Fix (b). The robust fix for the TaskRecord purge is a new typed verb `TXN_REMOVE_TASKS_BY_PACKAGE` in the proxy daemon (uses `IActivityTaskManager.getRecentTasks() + removeTask(taskId)` from the stable uid 2000 daemon context, atomic, no shell parse), specced in `docs/PLAN_v1.3.x_FULL_PROXY.md` §7.b — requires the daemon `PROTOCOL_VERSION` bump 2→3 and is scheduled for v1.3.x. **(Touched files.)** `app/build.gradle` (versionCode 199→200, versionName 1.2.8→1.2.9), `MainActivity.java` (`doKillApp` serialize move→kill via LaunchCallback ~50 LoC, `restoreBydDashboard` + `originCluster` pre-remove captured pkg from session set ~14 LoC total), `AdbLocalClient.java` (`verifyForceStop` active kill -9 escalation ~40 LoC), `docs/PLAN_v1.3.x_FULL_PROXY.md` (new §7.b describing the typed-verb robust fix). No new permissions, no new dependencies, no new strings, no protocol bump (still PROTOCOL_VERSION=2). DL3 / DL5 / DL2 behavioural contract identical except for the targeted bug fixes (DL2 has no cluster path so only verifyForceStop escalation can trigger there, but its `Platform.isDiLink2` ROM doesn't ship the typed verbs so the new code path is naturally inert). **(Validation plan.)** (a) DL3 Seal EU — install v1.2.9, start cluster projection, launch Waze on cluster, long-press Waze tile → Kill → confirm process dies (Waze gone from cluster). Open Recents on head unit: tile may still appear (known TaskRecord limitation, roadmap v1.3.x) but tapping it MUST relaunch Waze fresh (not resurrect with prior state on display 1) — proves the move-then-kill race is gone. (b) DL3 Seal EU — start cluster projection, launch Waze on cluster, tap Stop Projection → confirm cluster restored to BYD dashboard AND **NO Waze flash on head unit** (was: Waze popped fullscreen ~1 s after restore) → Fix (b) validated. Logcat tag `MainActivity` should show `restoreBydDashboard ... mSessionClusterPackages remove com.waze` BEFORE any `moveSessionAppsToMainDisplay` line. (c) DL5 testeur — same two scenarios, same expected results (shared MainActivity code path). (d) DL2 — install, navigate UI, no cluster path triggered, zero behaviour change. (e) Logcat tag `AdbLocalClient` — search for `verifyForceStop ... escalating kill -9`: should ONLY appear when force-stop typed verb didn't fully kill a process (rare on healthy device), confirming the escalation is dormant in nominal operation. |
| **1.2.8-build199** | 199 | **DL5 — keyboard bridge (manual + auto), accessibility on-boarding banner, resize sliders dynamic cluster routing, and head-unit display-0 hard guards (defense in depth).** Four coordinated DL5 payloads built on top of the v1.2.7 tactile fix, all gated behind `Platform.isDiLink5(Context)` so the DL3 production hot path is bit-for-bit identical. **(A) Keyboard bridge — manual ⌨ button + `KeyboardBridgeActivity`.** Field report (22/05/2026, post-v1.2.7): tactile injection on the DL5 cluster mirror now works (Yandex maps reacts to taps), BUT tapping an EditText inside Yandex does NOT pop up the system IME on the head unit. Root cause: WindowManager per-display IME isolation routes the focused window's IME request to display 3 (the 1×1 `shared_fission_bg_XDJAScreenProjection` shadow framebuffer) where it has nowhere to render — no clean override exists on API 32 without `MANAGE_DEVICE_POLICY_LOCAL_OVERRIDE` (System API unavailable on BYD-AUTO ROM). **Workaround design**: a translucent landscape `KeyboardBridgeActivity` is launched on display 0 (head unit) with a hidden `EditText` that triggers the native IME on tap; a `TextWatcher` computes the per-character diff and forwards each typed character to the cluster via `ClusterInputForwarder.injectKeyEvent(KeyEvent)` which already routes through the existing `TRANSACT_INJECT_KEY` daemon transaction (uid 2000, holds `INJECT_EVENTS`). `KeyCharacterMap.load(VIRTUAL_KEYBOARD).getEvents(char[])` is used to convert each typed character into a properly meta-state-aware `KeyEvent[]` sequence (handles SHIFT for capital letters and ALT for symbols correctly); a fallback `mapAsciiToKeyCode` covers degenerate ASCII when the KCM lookup fails. Backspace is translated to N×`KEYCODE_DEL` based on the `before` count from `TextWatcher`. The EditText is reset to empty after 80 characters to prevent unbounded growth. A new ⌨ button (`btn_keyboard_bridge`, 36 dp, tertiary-container colours) is inserted in the toolbar right after the existing Split-screen button, visibility-gated to DL5 only (`Platform.isDiLink5 → VISIBLE`, else `GONE`). **(B) Auto-popup via `ClusterImeWatcherService` (AccessibilityService).** Manual ⌨ taps work but the user-experience target is automatic pop-up the moment an EditText gains focus on the cluster. Android does not expose an in-process focus listener for foreign apps' windows, so the only platform-supported path is an AccessibilityService. New `com.byd.dashcast.ime.ClusterImeWatcherService` listens for `TYPE_VIEW_FOCUSED | TYPE_WINDOW_STATE_CHANGED` events (`feedbackGeneric`, `notificationTimeout=100`, `canRetrieveWindowContent=true`, declared in new `res/xml/accessibility_ime_watcher.xml`, registered in the manifest with `BIND_ACCESSIBILITY_SERVICE` and the `AccessibilityService` intent-filter). On every event the service runs a **five-guard filter chain** before doing anything: (1) `!Platform.isDiLink5(this) → return` (DL3 strictly never triggers — refreshed on every event in case the platform flag flips at runtime), (2) `eventType != TYPE_VIEW_FOCUSED → return`, (3) `!ClusterService.sIsRunning → return` (hard gate: only react while the DashCast cluster projection is actually live — if DashCast is closed the service is registered with the system but does NOTHING), (4) `event.getDisplayId() <= 0 → return` (strictly secondary displays only, blocks display 0 and the unknown-id (-1) case on pre-API-30 fallbacks), (5) `node not editable || pkg == getPackageName() → return` (no self-trigger on the bridge's own EditText). Additional safety: 600 ms debounce via `mLastLaunchAt`, and `KeyboardBridgeActivity.isShowing()` static check (set in `onStart`/`onStop`/`onDestroy`) to skip launch when the bridge is already visible. Every callback path is wrapped in `try { … } catch (Throwable t) { AppLogger.e(…); }` — the service never crashes the host app. The launch sends an intent with `EXTRA_AUTO_OPENED=true` so the bridge can distinguish manual vs. auto invocations if needed in future builds. **(C) On-boarding banner for the AccessibilityService.** Android requires the user to enable an AccessibilityService manually in Settings (no programmatic enable possible by design). A new `MaterialCardView card_ime_a11y_banner` (tertiary-container, corner 12 dp, elevation 0, hidden by default) is inserted in `MainActivity` right after the top bar; it shows on first launch when (a) `Platform.isDiLink5(this)`, (b) the service is not yet in `Settings.Secure.ENABLED_ACCESSIBILITY_SERVICES` (parsed colon-separated, comparing both `flattenToString` and `flattenToShortString`), AND (c) the user has not dismissed it (`PREF_IME_BANNER_DISMISSED`). Three actions: **Enable** (deep-links to `Settings.ACTION_ACCESSIBILITY_SETTINGS` for the user to flip the toggle, then Toast `Clavier automatique actif ✓` is shown on return to `MainActivity` if enabled), **Later** (session-hide only, banner reappears next launch), **Dismiss** (persists `PREF_IME_BANNER_DISMISSED=true`, banner never shown again unless prefs cleared). The banner is fully no-op on DL3 (`Platform.isDiLink5=false` → never shown). All MainActivity wiring is wrapped in `try { … } catch (Throwable t) { AppLogger.e(…); }`. **(D) DL5 resize sliders — dynamic cluster routing.** Field finding: the in-app per-app resize panel (`btn_toggle_resize` → SeekBars W/H → Apply) was a no-op on DL5. Two hard-coded bugs: (i) `MainActivity.btnResizeApply` and `autoApplyInsetsIfNeeded` both issued `wm overscan W,H,W,H -d 1` (cluster id on DL3 only — on DL5 the cluster lives on display 3 or 4, so the command silently failed), (ii) `ClusterService.resizeActiveTask` hard-coded the freeform `Rect` to `1920 - insetH, 720 - insetV` (DL3 cluster size — on DL5 the composed cluster face may differ). Fix: both MainActivity call sites now resolve the cluster display id dynamically via `ClusterService.getDisplayId()` (which delegates to `DashboardDisplayHelper.getKnownClusterDisplayId()`, the same source already used by every other cluster-routed code path in the app), and skip the `wm overscan` call entirely when `clusterId <= 0` (no cluster connected — never silently retarget to display 0). `ClusterService.resizeActiveTask` and the inline post-`moveTaskToDisplay` resize block now both compute bounds from `mInputForwarder.getClusterWidth()/getClusterHeight()` (set live from `Display.getSize()` in `setClusterDisplay`), with explicit fallback to `1920×720` if either getter returns 0 — DL3 behaviour stays bit-for-bit identical (DL3 cluster IS 1920×720, fallback path even matches the old hard-coded values). On DL5 the bounds now reflect the actual cluster dimensions reported by the system. **(E) Head-unit display-0 hard guards (defense in depth).** Concern raised: even with the per-call-site `clusterId > 0` checks in (D), a single regressed caller (future refactor, off-path code, hand-typed shell command via the dev console) could still ship a `wm overscan|size|density … -d 0` to the head-unit display 0 and shrink the main UI (the v1.2.5 DL2 incident playbook). Three new layers, all independent: **(E1)** New `ShellGateway.WM_DISPLAY_ZERO` regex (`^\s*wm\s+(?:overscan|size|density)\b.*\s-d\s+0\b.*$`) blocks any `wm` verb explicitly targeting display 0, checked at the top of `execShellWithResult` BEFORE both the proxy-ON typed-verb path AND the legacy `AdbLocalClient` fallback — neither can reach the system. Blocked commands emit `AppLogger.e("BLOCKED wm verb on display 0 (head unit): …")` and the caller's `Callback.onError("blocked: wm command targets display 0 (head unit)")`. **(E2)** New abort in `ClusterService.resizeActiveTask`: if `mDisplayHelper.getKnownClusterDisplayId() <= 0` at call time, log `resizeActiveTask aborted: no cluster display connected (taskId=… pkg=…)` and return without invoking `IActivityTaskManager.resizeTask`. Without this, a stale `taskId` that happens to live on display 0 (because a prior cluster-move failed silently) would have applied DL3 freeform bounds to the head-unit task. **(E3)** The two existing `MainActivity` `clusterId > 0` guards from (D) form the outermost layer at the UI entry points. Coverage matrix: to reach display 0 a caller would have to simultaneously bypass the UI-level guard, the cluster-connected guard in `resizeActiveTask`, AND the regex at the shell gateway — three independent layers, no single point of failure. DL3 zero behaviour change: `clusterId` is always `1`, the regex never matches `-d 1`, the `getKnownClusterDisplayId` abort never fires in nominal operation (cluster is connected before any resize is invoked). **(F) Behavioural contract.** DL3 production (BYD Seal EU, daily-driver reference): (a) ⌨ button is `GONE`, AccessibilityService is registered with the system but on-event-check `!isDiLink5 → return` short-circuits before any work — no behavioural change of the IME. (b) Banner is hidden (`isDiLink5=false`). (c) Resize sliders issue `wm overscan H,V,H,V -d 1` (resolved from `getDisplayId()`, identical to the old hard-coded value), `resizeTask` bounds computed as `1920 - insetH, 720 - insetV` (identical to old hard-coded values via the `getClusterWidth/Height` fallback). (d) Display-0 guards are no-ops (never triggered, cluster id is 1 on DL3). DL5 testeur: (a) ⌨ button visible, manual tap → bridge appears on head unit, typing forwards to Yandex/Maps/anything on cluster. (b) Once the user enables the AccessibilityService in Settings, focusing any EditText on the cluster auto-launches the bridge within ~100 ms. (c) Banner walks the user through enabling the service on first launch. (d) Sliders now actually resize the cluster app (was a silent no-op before). (e) Display-0 protections are a strict superset of the v1.2.5 DL2 hard-ban — same defense-in-depth design. DL2: completely unaffected — DashCast cluster surface never runs on DL2, the AccessibilityService still checks `isDiLink5` first so it stays inert if accidentally enabled. **(G) Touched files.** `MyBYDApp/app/build.gradle` (versionCode 196→199, versionName 1.2.7→1.2.8). NEW: `KeyboardBridgeActivity.java`, `ime/ClusterImeWatcherService.java`, `res/xml/accessibility_ime_watcher.xml`. MODIFIED: `AndroidManifest.xml` (+2 component registrations), `MainActivity.java` (+keyboard button + banner + dynamic cluster id in 2 resize sites), `ClusterService.java` (+`getInstance()` + `clusterId <= 0` abort in `resizeActiveTask` + dynamic `cw/ch` bounds), `dashboard/ClusterInputForwarder.java` (+public `injectKeyEvent(KeyEvent)`), `beta/ShellGateway.java` (+`WM_DISPLAY_ZERO` regex + guard), `res/layout/activity_main.xml` (+keyboard button + banner card), `res/values/strings.xml` (+13 string keys, FR fallback for other locales pending). No new permissions, no new dependencies, no protocol bump (PROTOCOL_VERSION=2 unchanged). **(H) Validation plan.** (a) DL5 testeur — install, start cluster projection, open Yandex on cluster: (a1) tap ⌨ → bridge appears on head unit, typing forwards each character to Yandex search bar. (a2) Enable AccessibilityService in Settings, tap Yandex search bar → bridge auto-launches within ~100 ms. (a3) Adjust → slide W/H → Apply: Yandex window on cluster recomputes bounds (head unit unaffected — visual proof display 0 is untouched). (a4) Banner appears on first launch, Enable → Toast `Clavier automatique actif ✓`. (b) DL3 Seal EU — ⌨ button NOT visible, banner NOT shown, resize sliders behave exactly as v1.2.7 (cluster id resolves to 1, bounds 1920×720, command `-d 1`), `ClusterImeWatcherService` logcat never shows any post-`!isDiLink5` line. (c) DL2 — everything works exactly as v1.2.7 (no cluster, no ⌨, no banner, AccessibilityService inert). |
| **1.2.7-build196** | 196 | **DL5 tactile fix — displayId override 3→2 (and 4→2) for daemon touch injection (symmetric to v1.2.6's layerStack override), plus daemon `injectMotion`/`injectKey` first-event + failure instrumentation in M7 log.** Field test of v1.2.6 on DL5 testeur (22/05/2026, user log `byd_report_20260522_202932.log`, 1230 lines) confirmed (A) preview now shows the actual composed cluster content (layerStack 3→2 override works as designed, Yandex visible in the in-app TextureView in real-time) but (B) touch injection still does NOT reach Yandex when the user taps either the preview surface OR the fullscreen mirror surface. Log evidence: `ClusterInputForwarder Cluster dimensions: 1920x720 displayId=3`, `Daemon Binder connected — touch/key injection via uid=2000`, daemon holds `android.permission.INJECT_EVENTS`, no `injectMotion failed` in logcat, no `touch →` debug line (filtered from report) — daemon was targeting `setDisplayId(3)` (the shadow render display where Yandex's window technically lives per `mDisplayId=3 rootTaskId=79 Session{… 7775:u0a10124}`). On DL3 the same architecture works because there's no fission split — displayId returned by `Display.getDisplayId()` is the same one the InputDispatcher routes to. On DL5, the WindowManager exposes a SEPARATE `mDisplayId=2` (also visible in `mWindowsForAccessibilityObserver={…, 2=…, 3=…, 4=…}`) backing the `fission_bg_XDJAScreenProjection` 1920×720 composite — which is where the *user actually sees* the apps. The hypothesis (to be empirically validated by this build) is that on DL5 the InputDispatcher either (a) routes injected events on displayId=3 into the 1×1 framebufferSpace and they get clipped, or (b) refuses to route into PRESENTATION displays with degenerate framebuffer dims, or (c) the apps' WindowManager-side window on displayId=3 isn't actually receiving touch events because it lives in a SurfaceFlinger layer composited away by containerservice into displayId=2's surface. **(1) `ClusterMirrorManager.applyDl5DisplayIdOverride(Context, int)` — sibling helper to v1.2.6's `applyDl5LayerStackOverride`.** Same shape, same DL3-safe contract: `Platform.get().isDiLink5(ctx)` wrapped in try/catch (any failure returns input unchanged), DL3 returns input unchanged, DL5 rewrites `3` and `4` to `2` with an explicit `AppLogger.i DL5 override: displayId N → 2 (touch injection on composed cluster face)` log line. Wired in `startMirrorViaDaemon` right before `data.writeInt(clusterDisplayId)` — the value of `sClusterDisplayId` stored statically inside `MirrorDaemon` (and used by `injectMotion` for `MotionEvent.setDisplayId`) now becomes `2` on DL5 instead of `3`. **(2) `MirrorDaemon.injectMotion` / `injectKey` — first-event + failure instrumentation via `out()`.** v1.2.6 made the daemon's setupMirror trace appear in `/data/local/tmp/mirrordaemon_latest.log` (the file M7 reads), but the `injectMotion`/`injectKey` paths still only logged failures to logcat via `Log.w`. New `volatile boolean sMotionFirstLogged`/`sKeyFirstLogged` flags (reset in `setupMirror` start so each session re-arms once) make the very first successful injection emit `out("injectMotion FIRST OK displayId=N setDisplayIdAvail=true|false action=K x=X y=Y ret=R")` to the file log; any subsequent exception emits `out("injectMotion EXCEPTION displayId=N action=K err=ClassName: message")`. The pre-check path (`ev==null || sInputManager==null`) also emits a one-shot diagnostic. Same instrumentation pattern for `injectKey`. Next M7 capture on DL5 will tell us: did the override take effect (displayId=2 in the FIRST OK line), did `setDisplayId` reflection survive (sSetDisplayIdAvail=true), did InputManager accept the event (no EXCEPTION line). **(3) Behavioural contract for DL3 (production reference).** Zero risk by construction: `applyDl5DisplayIdOverride` returns input unchanged on `isDiLink5=false`, so `clusterDisplayId` written to the daemon's MIRROR_START parcel stays exactly what `Display.getDisplayId()` returned on DL3 (typically `2` already, no override applies). The daemon `out()` additions are pure log writes; the `Log.w` calls are preserved. No public API change, no protocol bump (`PROTOCOL_VERSION=2`). **(4) Touched files.** `dashboard/ClusterMirrorManager.java` (+`applyDl5DisplayIdOverride` helper sibling, +1 call site in `startMirrorViaDaemon` — ~22 LoC), `daemon/MirrorDaemon.java` (+2 volatile flags, reset in `setupMirror`, instrumented `injectMotion` and `injectKey` — ~30 LoC), `build.gradle` (versionCode 195→196, versionName 1.2.6→1.2.7), `CHANGELOG.md`. No new permissions, no new dependencies, no new layouts/strings. **(5) Validation plan.** (a) DL5 testeur — install v1.2.7, activate cluster, launch Yandex/Maps on cluster, then **tap on the in-app preview surface** (or enter fullscreen via the 📺 button and tap there): expected — Yandex now responds to touch (map pans, buttons react). Logcat tag `ClusterMirrorManager` should contain `DL5 override: displayId 3 → 2 (touch injection on composed cluster face)` exactly once per mirror session, and `startMirrorViaDaemon ✓ layerStack=2 1920×720 displayId=2` (note the `displayId=2` instead of `=3` like v1.2.6). (b) If still not working — open Diag → Mirror → `Lancer tous`, M7 detail will now show the first `injectMotion FIRST OK displayId=2 setDisplayIdAvail=true action=0 x=… y=… ret=true/false` line. If `ret=false` → InputManager refused, escalate to either trying `displayId=0` or routing via the WindowManager's `mInputMonitorPerDisplay` channel. If `setDisplayIdAvail=false` → reflection failed on this ROM, the event went to displayId=0 by default → DashCast self-consumed it (explains the silent failure on v1.2.6). If no `injectMotion FIRST OK` line at all → the OnTouchListener on the TextureView never fired (layout issue) — pivot to layout debugging. (c) DL3 Seal EU — install, activate cluster, run any cluster app, confirm fullscreen mirror tactile still works exactly as before. Logcat should NOT contain any `DL5 override: displayId` line. (d) DL2 — install, navigate UI, no cluster path triggered, zero behaviour change. Done. |
| **1.2.6-build195** | 195 | **Two coordinated payloads — (A) Beta Engine proxy daemon perf passes P1-P4 + (B) DL5 mirror investigation (M10 fix, daemon logging, layerStack override).** **(A) Beta Engine proxy daemon — 4 perf passes (P1-P4), zero behaviour change, lock-free hot paths.** Audit of the production proxy daemon (`ProxyDaemonMain` 352 LoC + `Phase4Verbs` 287 LoC + `BetaProxyClient` 576 LoC + `ShellGateway` 208 LoC) surfaced four micro-perf wins that compound on the cluster-projection hot paths without touching the wire protocol, the bootstrap script, the reflection cache, or any business logic. Production semantics preserved bit-for-bit on DL3 (BYD Seal EU production reference) and DL5 (BYD-AUTO testeur): no API removed, no return value changed, no callback signature changed, no `PROTOCOL_VERSION` bump. **(P1) Lock-free hot-path verbs in `BetaProxyClient`.** Field `sBinder` promoted to `volatile`; `synchronized (LOCK)` removed from `setOverscan`, `getPidsByPackage`, `autoContainerSendInfo`, `forceStopPackage`, `runShell`, `runPhase4Probes`, `ping`. Volatile `sDaemonUid` / `sDaemonPid` / `sDaemonVer` make the public getters (`getCallerUid`, `getDaemonPid`, `getProtocolVersion`) lockless too. `connect()`, the receiver and `handshake()` still take `LOCK` for sBinder publication — the only place it was strictly needed. **Why safe.** `IBinder.transact()` is documented thread-safe (it dispatches into the daemon's binder thread pool which already serialises per-transaction); `Parcel.obtain()` returns from a thread-safe pool; the only shared mutable state read by the hot paths is `sBinder` itself which is now correctly published via `volatile`. The `sBinder = null` cleanup on `RemoteException` is preserved in every verb (single volatile assignment, race-free) so the fallback contract in `ShellGateway` (typed-verb fail → generic `runShell` → legacy `AdbLocalClient`) is bit-for-bit identical. **Why it matters.** The resize SeekBar in Settings fires ~30 `setOverscan` per second while dragging; the reconcile loop in `MainActivity` fires `getPidsByPackage` every ~5 s during a projection session. Under the old serial mutex, they queued behind any in-flight `runShell` (e.g. an `am start` that takes 500 ms+), which manifested as visible UI lag on the slider and stale state on the reconcile tick. Lockless verbs let them dispatch in parallel through the daemon's binder pool. **(P2) `DeathRecipient` hooked on the live binder → `isBinderAlive()` instead of `pingBinder()` in `isConnected()`.** The old `isConnected()` performed a full Binder IPC roundtrip on every call (`pingBinder()` is *not* a local flag check — it issues `IBinder.PING_TRANSACTION` to the remote process, ~5 ms on this hardware). Every typed verb routes through `isConnected()` first via `ShellGateway`, so each verb cost two IPC: one to ping, one to transact. Build 195 hooks `sBinder.linkToDeath(sDeath, 0)` in the broadcast receiver right after the live binder arrives; the static `DeathRecipient` clears `sBinder` (under `LOCK`) as soon as the kernel notifies us of the daemon's death. Now `isConnected()` can rely on the cheap local `isBinderAlive()` flag instead. Per-verb latency drops from ~10 ms to ~5 ms (×2). Receiver also unhooks the previous death recipient before swapping in a fresh binder, and best-effort-catches `RemoteException` from `linkToDeath` if the binder died between `pingBinder` (stale-broadcast check) and `linkToDeath` (vanishingly unlikely race). The `pingBinder` call in the receiver — used to discard stale broadcasts whose binder is already dead — is kept, because `isBinderAlive()` on a never-pinged binder returns true unconditionally and would let stale references through. **Why safe.** `DeathRecipient` is a pure additive hook with no observable side-effect when the daemon stays alive; if `linkToDeath` ever fails, the next typed verb's `isBinderAlive()` still returns the right answer (false post-death), exactly like the pre-P2 `pingBinder()`-on-every-call behaviour. **(P3) `ThreadLocal<byte[]>` reusable scratch in `Phase4Verbs.getPidsByPackage`.** Previously every call allocated a fresh `byte[256]` per `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` scan iteration — ~241 allocations per scan on a typical BYD device with 241 live processes. The daemon's Binder thread pool consists of a small set of long-lived threads, so a `ThreadLocal` yields one buffer per thread for the daemon's lifetime: zero allocation per scan, zero GC pressure on the reconcile tick. The loop body and matching semantics are byte-for-byte unchanged. **Why safe.** ThreadLocal scoping matches the binder pool's thread reuse pattern; the buffer is overwritten on every `FileInputStream.read(buf)` so cross-iteration leak is impossible. **(P4) `ByteArrayOutputStream` in `ProxyDaemonMain.runShell`.** Replaces the per-line `BufferedReader.readLine()` + `StringBuilder.append('\n').append(line)` pattern. The legacy code stripped trailing newlines via `readLine()` then re-added separators (~3-4 `String` allocations per line + 1 `StringBuilder` grow); on a 300-line `dumpsys SurfaceFlinger` that was ~1000 transient allocations. P4 reads the full child-process stream in 4 KiB chunks into a single `ByteArrayOutputStream` then decodes once with `toString("UTF-8")`. Trailing `\r`/`\n` are stripped after decode to **exactly reproduce** the legacy line-joiner semantics (which never appended a final `\n`) — verified character-by-character against the old code path. **Why safe.** Same UTF-8 decoding (Android default charset), same trailing-newline handling, same `redirectErrorStream(true)` capture order, same `ExecResult.exit` from `p.waitFor()`. The byte-stream pipeline is the same one used by `Process.getInputStream()` internally; the old `InputStreamReader` was just adding a decode-per-line layer on top. **(5) Wire protocol.** `PROTOCOL_VERSION` *unchanged* (still `"2"`). All TXN codes unchanged. Parcel layout per-TXN unchanged. An app-update that lands while a daemon from build 192-194 is still alive will transparently talk to the old daemon until the next bootstrap kills it (`ps -A \| grep '[d]ashcast_proxy' \| kill -9`). No incompat possible. **(6) Touched files.** `app/build.gradle` (versionCode 194→195, versionName 1.2.5→1.2.6), `beta/BetaProxyClient.java` (volatile fields, `DeathRecipient`, `isBinderAlive()` in `isConnected()`, lockless verbs + getters — ~45 LoC modified, no public API change), `beta/proxy/Phase4Verbs.java` (`ThreadLocal<byte[]>` scratch — +6 LoC), `beta/proxy/ProxyDaemonMain.java` (`runShell` rewritten with `ByteArrayOutputStream` — ~20 LoC modified), `CHANGELOG.md`. No new permissions, no new dependencies, no new layouts, no new strings. **(7) Behavioural contract.** DL3 production: byte-for-byte identical sequence of binder transactions and shell outputs as build 194 (we don't change *what* runs, only *how* the dispatcher / parser work). DL5 testeur: same. DL2: completely unaffected (Beta Engine is disabled by default and any production resize is hard-blocked by the build-191 DL2 guard which sits *before* `ShellGateway`). M10 LIVE probe from build 194 still passes through `AdbLocalClient` (not `BetaProxyClient`), so its evidence shape is unchanged. **(8) Validation plan.** (a) Install on DL3 Seal EU — launch DashCast, open Settings, drag the overscan SeekBar smoothly from 0 to 80 to 0: expect (i) zero visible lag (P1+P2 → ~2× faster per-tick), (ii) per-tick logcat tag `BetaProxyClient` shows `setOverscan typed ok (~3-5 ms)` instead of 8-12 ms in build 194. Start projection, confirm cluster lights up identically to build 194 (no visual regression), force-stop projection, confirm restore identical. (b) Install on DL5 testeur — launch DashCast, open Diag → Mirror tab, run `Lancer tous`: expect identical M1-M10 output as build 194 (zero regression). Then enable Beta Engine in Settings → run the DL5 D-suite: identical PASS/FAIL pattern as build 194. (c) Install on DL2 testeur — launch DashCast, navigate UI, ensure Settings `Marges` card still hidden, no `DL2 BLOCK` spurious lines in logcat. (d) Optional micro-bench in Diag → Logs: `BetaProxyClient.ping()` reported latency should be the same as build 194 (this is an explicit IPC ping, unaffected by P1/P2). Done. **(B) DL5 mirror investigation — 3 surgical fixes for the DL5 black-preview symptom (gated on `Platform.isDiLink5`, DL3 strictly untouched).** First DL5 in-car test of build 192-194 mirror path (user log `byd_report_20260522_200736.log`, 1230 lines) confirmed end-to-end routing works: `auto_container sendInfo(1000,16)` opens the cluster projection tunnel and `am start --display 3 ru.yandex.yandexmaps` lands Yandex on the cluster display (M10 STEP 6: `mDisplayId=3 rootTaskId=50 mSession=Session{... 17239:u0a10124}`), AND the app-side mirror logs `startMirrorViaDaemon ✓ layerStack=3 1920×720 displayId=3` — yet the in-app preview surface stays black. Root-cause hypothesis from the SF dump (M5): DL5 fission architecture has THREE virtual displays for the cluster — `shared_fission_bg_XDJAScreenProjection_0` (layerStack=3, framebufferSpace bounds `1×1`), `shared_fission_bg_XDJAScreenProjection_1` (layerStack=4, framebufferSpace bounds `1×1`), and `fission_bg_XDJAScreenProjection` (layerStack=2, framebufferSpace bounds `1920×720`). Apps render on layerStack 3/4 (shadow displays with degenerate 1×1 framebuffer used as logical render targets), then `com.byd.containerservice` re-composes the selected slot into layerStack=2 which is the actual 1920×720 surface piped to the physical cluster panel. Build 195's app-side and daemon-side mirror was capturing layerStack=3 because that's what `Display.getLayerStack()` returns for the DisplayManager id we're given — resulting in a 1×1 black buffer in the TextureView. **(B1) DL5 layerStack override 3→2 (and 4→2) in `ClusterMirrorManager`.** New private helper `applyDl5LayerStackOverride(Context ctx, int detectedLayerStack)` wraps `Platform.get().isDiLink5(ctx)` in a try/catch (any reflection failure returns the input unchanged so DL3 path is bit-for-bit identical); on confirmed DL5, layerStack 3 or 4 is rewritten to 2 with an explicit `AppLogger.i DL5 override: layerStack N → 2 (mirror composed fission output)` line, any other value (1, 5+) is passed through untouched. Helper is called inside both `startMirror(Context, Display, Surface, int, int)` (app-uid direct SurfaceControl path) and `startMirrorViaDaemon(Context, IBinder, Display, Surface, int, int)` (daemon-uid path) right after `getLayerStack()` reflection — symmetric coverage so either fallback chain captures the correct compositor output. Both method signatures gained a leading `Context` parameter; sole caller is `MainActivity.attemptStartMirror` (2 sites), both updated to pass `this`. **(B2) Daemon-side mirror setup logging (`MirrorDaemon.setupMirror`).** All pre-build-195 mirror traces in the daemon went through `Log.i(TAG, …)` (logcat-only), so when the on-device M7 diagnostic test reads `/data/local/tmp/mirrordaemon_latest.log` (which only captures `out()` lines) the file showed only the daemon's startup banner — no setup chain. Build 195 mirrors the critical-path `Log.i` calls of `setupMirror` to `out()`: `setupMirror BEGIN layerStack=N cluster=WxH view=WxH surface=valid=true/false`, `setupMirror createDisplay OK token=…` (or `setupMirror FAIL createDisplay returned null (DL5 SurfaceControl quirk?)`), `setupMirror SF dump (layerStack=N): …` (or `(empty — token NOT in SurfaceFlinger!)` when the post-setup grep returns nothing), `setupMirror DONE ok=true layerStack=N dst=WxH off=(X,Y)`, and `setupMirror EXCEPTION: ClassName: message` on any reflection failure. Now the next in-car M7 capture will show the full daemon chain in the file — if mirror still fails, we can read exactly which step returned null or threw. **(B3) M10 LIVE probe fixes (`MirrorTestRunner.runM10`).** The build-194 M10 contained two known-bad hard-codings that made the test less useful on DL5: STEP 2 / STEP 7 issued `service call AutoContainer 2 …` (PascalCase, the legacy DL3 binder service name), but the cluster service on DL5 is registered as `auto_container` (snake_case, confirmed in production by `ClusterManager`'s prod path) — STEP 2 reported `Service AutoContainer does not exist` and STEP 7's cleanup `sendInfo(18)+sendInfo(0)` was a no-op. STEP 4/5 launched `com.android.settings/.Settings` (BYD ROM doesn't ship this exact AOSP activity name — `Activity class does not exist`). Build 195 replaces `AutoContainer` with `auto_container` (3 occurrences) and `com.android.settings/.Settings` with `com.android.launcher3/.Launcher` (2 occurrences; launcher3 is universally installed on AOSP and visible in M3 dumps). M10 doc comment updated. The test is still ALWAYS PASS (manual analysis of STEP 3 framebufferSpace diff and STEP 6 routing), but next run will produce real sendInfo Parcel hex and real `am start` Status/TotalTime/WaitTime + matching rootTaskId in STEP 6 — no more two-bugs-cancelling-out. **(B4) Behavioural contract for DL3 (production reference).** Zero risk by construction: `applyDl5LayerStackOverride` returns input unchanged when `Platform.isDiLink5` returns false, daemon `out()` additions are pure log writes (no logic), `Context` param addition is a pure signature widening with all callers updated. The only DL3-observable change is in M10 (Diag-only test, never on the runtime hot path): on DL3 `auto_container` may or may not exist as a binder service name and `launcher3` is the universal launcher → the diag result strings will differ from build 194 but the test still PASSes and no production code path is affected. **(B5) Touched files for (B).** `dashboard/ClusterMirrorManager.java` (+`Platform` import, +`Context ctx` param on `startMirror` and `startMirrorViaDaemon`, +`applyDl5LayerStackOverride` helper, 2 call-sites of the helper — ~30 LoC), `daemon/MirrorDaemon.java` (+6 `out()` calls in `setupMirror` — ~12 LoC), `mirror/MirrorTestRunner.java` (3× `AutoContainer`→`auto_container` + 2× `com.android.settings/.Settings`→`com.android.launcher3/.Launcher` + doc comment refresh — ~10 LoC), `MainActivity.java` (+2 `this,` args in `attemptStartMirror` mirror startup — 2 LoC). No new permissions, no new dependencies, no new strings/layouts. **(B6) Validation plan for (B).** (a) DL5 testeur — install, activate cluster, launch any app on display 3 (Yandex, Maps, etc.), **observe in-app preview surface DURING active mirror (don't open Diag first)** — expected: preview now shows the actual cluster content (layerStack=2 composed fission output) instead of black. If still black: open Diag → Mirror → `Lancer tous`, M7 will now contain the full daemon setupMirror chain (createDisplay token, SF dump result, projection params) — paste in next issue. (b) DL5 testeur — re-run M10: STEP 2 should now show a successful `Parcel(00000000 00000001)` from `auto_container`, STEP 4/5 should show `Starting: Intent { ... cmp=com.android.launcher3/.Launcher }` with a real Status/TotalTime, STEP 6 should still show the `rootTaskId=N mSession=...` evidence as in build 194 but now correlated with launcher3 instead of "does not exist". (c) DL3 Seal EU — install, activate cluster, launch Yandex/Waze on cluster: expected zero behaviour change vs build 194 (mirror works exactly as before, `applyDl5LayerStackOverride` short-circuits via `Platform.isDiLink5=false`). Logcat tag `ClusterMirrorManager` should NOT contain any `DL5 override` line on DL3. (d) DL2 testeur — install, navigate UI: zero impact (DL2 has no cluster display so mirror code path never runs). | **1.2.5-build194** | 194 | **Diag → Mirror — new M10 LIVE cluster-routing probe (sendInfo 16 + am start --display 3/4).** Builds 192-193 gave us read-only SF / WindowManager snapshots of the DL5 cluster topology and confirmed visually that `sendInfo(1000, 16)` opens a black projection window on the cluster, but no single run captured the BEFORE/AFTER framebufferSpace transition AND the activity-routing outcome in the same evidence file. M10 closes that gap end-to-end in one shot. **(1) Test logic.** STEP 1 snapshots `dumpsys SurfaceFlinger` filtered on `DisplayDevice{|framebufferSpace=ProjectionSpace|layerStack=` (head -120) — gives the BEFORE state of every fission/XDJA display, expected mostly `1×1` slots for displays 3/4. STEP 2 fires `service call AutoContainer 2 i32 1000 i32 16 s16 ""` via ADB-local shell (uid 2000, the only Binder path that works on DL5 since the app-uid `getService("autocontainer")` returns SIGNATURE_NO_MATCH). STEP 3 waits 2 s then re-snapshots the same grep — the display whose `framebufferSpace` flips from `1×1` to `1920×720` is the one the cluster compositor latches onto. STEPS 4 & 5 fire `am start-activity -W --display 3` then `--display 4` with `com.android.settings/.Settings` (neutral, multi-window-friendly system app, always present) and capture the `-W` synchronous result (Status / TotalTime / WaitTime). STEP 6 dumps `dumpsys window` filtered on `Display: mDisplayId=|mCurrentFocus=|mFocusedApp=|rootTaskId=` to confirm which display now actually hosts the Settings task. STEP 7 cleans up: `am force-stop com.android.settings` + `sendInfo 18` (close projection) + `sendInfo 0` (restore Qt native video stream). All seven step outputs are concatenated into the single test detail blob shipped via `Envoyer (.log)` → Telegram. **(2) Status semantics.** M10 always PASS — analysis is manual (compare STEP 1 vs STEP 3 framebufferSpace per display, then STEP 6 routing); a hard PASS/FAIL would have required hard-coding an expected display id, but the whole point of this test is to *discover* that id. The result message reads `live routing run complete — compare STEP 1 vs STEP 3, then STEP 6`. **(3) Side-effect contract.** Two non-trivial side-effects, both pre-validated as user-safe: (a) `sendInfo 16` opens a black projection window on the cluster for ~6 s (the user has visually confirmed this in build 192 D28 and it is harmless), (b) the Settings activity briefly launches and is force-stopped. STEP 7 always runs even if STEPS 4/5 fail, so the cluster is always returned to its baseline. No production code path is invoked (the suite stays read-only at the architecture level). **(4) Touched files.** `mirror/MirrorTestRunner.java` (+1 catalog entry, +1 dispatcher case, +1 `runM10` method ~85 LoC, +1 reused grep constant), `build.gradle` (versionCode 193→194, versionName 1.2.4→1.2.5). No new permissions, dependencies, layouts, strings or drawables — M10 reuses the existing `runShellSync` helper, `AdbLocalClient.executeShellWithResult` infrastructure, and the existing `include_diag_mirror.xml` row layout. **(5) Validation plan.** (a) Install on DL5 device. Open DashCast → Diag → swipe to Mirror tab → tap `Lancer tous`. Wait ~25 s for M10 (longest test in the suite due to the 2 s + 1 s + 1 s waits). (b) Tap `Envoyer (.log)` → Telegram. (c) In the report look for `[PASS] M10` then under `--- detail ---` read STEP 1 vs STEP 3: ONE of `fission_bg_XDJAScreenProjection_0/1/2` should have flipped from `framebufferSpace=ProjectionSpace(start=Rect(0, 0, 1, 1)` to `=Rect(0, 0, 1920, 720)`. That display's `layerStack=` value is the cluster compositor's target layerStack. Then STEP 6 should show `rootTaskId=...` for a `mDisplayId=3` or `mDisplayId=4` entry hosting `com.android.settings/.Settings` — confirming the activity-routing side of the pipeline. (d) On DL3 / DL2 devices the test will run too but STEP 2 will likely return `Service AutoContainer does not exist` (DL3) or hit the DL2 single-display ROM (no cluster compositor) — the test still PASSES with the empty-output evidence, which is itself useful (proves AutoContainer is DL5-only). |

Zero changes to `MirrorDaemon`, `ClusterMirrorManager`, `ClusterService` or any cluster touch/launch path. DL3 mirror unchanged. DL5 mirror unchanged. Existing 9 Diag tabs unchanged (DL5 D30 keeps its broader SF dump, M-suite is a more curated mirror-focused subset). **(4) Build.** `versionCode` 192 → 193, `versionName` 1.2.3 → 1.2.4.
